transferor, within five Business Days of receipt. Transfers will be effected without charge, except for any
tax or other governmental charge imposed in relation to the transfer. No transfer will be registered during
the 15 days ending on the due date for any payment of principal or interest on the Notes.

**(f) Selling restrictions.** The Notes may be offered, sold and transferred only to professional investors in
the United Kingdom and the European Economic Area, and only in compliance with the restrictions described in
section 20. Each purchaser of an interest in a Note is deemed to have made the representations set out in
section 20.

## Condition 2 — Status and ranking

**(a) Status.** The Notes are direct, secured, limited recourse and unconditional obligations of the Issuer,
secured by the Security described in Condition 3 and recoverable only as provided in Condition 11.

**(b) Ranking within a Class.** The Notes of each Class rank *pari passu* and rateably, without any preference
or priority among themselves, for all purposes.

**(c) Ranking between Classes.** Before the delivery of an Enforcement Notice, payments of interest and
principal on the Notes are made in the order set out in the Pre-Enforcement Priority of Payments (section 15).
In that order, interest on the Class A Notes is paid before interest on the Class B Notes, which is paid
before interest on the Class C Notes, which is paid before interest on the Class D Notes. Principal is paid
to all Classes *pro rata* to their Principal Amount Outstanding until the first Payment Date following the
occurrence of a Trigger Event and sequentially thereafter, as described in section 4 and section 16. After
the delivery of an Enforcement Notice, the Class A Notes rank in priority to the Class B Notes, the Class B
Notes in priority to the Class C Notes and the Class C Notes in priority to the Class D Notes, as to both
interest and principal, under the Post-Enforcement Priority of Payments.

**(d) Payments to other Secured Creditors.** The Issuer's obligations to the other Secured Creditors rank in
the order set out in the Pre-Enforcement Priority of Payments and the Post-Enforcement Priority of Payments.
The amounts due to the Servicer and the Trustee rank ahead of all payments on the Notes. Deferred consideration
payable to the Seller ranks behind all payments on the Notes.

**(e) Conflicts of interest between Classes.** The Trust Deed requires the Trustee, in exercising its powers,
trusts, authorities, duties and discretions, to have regard to the interests of the holders of each Class as a
class. Where, in the opinion of the Trustee, there is a conflict between the interests of the holders of two or
more Classes, the Trustee is required to have regard only to the interests of the holders of the Most Senior
Class then outstanding, and it will have no liability to the holders of any other Class for so doing. Where
there is a conflict between the interests of the Noteholders and those of any other Secured Creditor, the
Trustee is required to have regard only to the interests of the Noteholders. The Trustee is not required to
have regard to the interests of individual Noteholders arising from their particular circumstances, including
their place of residence or their tax position.

**(f) Retained Notes.** The Originator will hold, on the Closing Date and for the life of the transaction,
Notes of each Class equal to 5% of the nominal value of that Class, as its retained interest in the form of a
vertical slice described in section 18 (the "Retained Notes"). The Retained Notes rank *pari passu* with the
other Notes of the same Class and carry the same rights, save that, for as long as the Originator or any of its
affiliates holds them, they are disregarded for the purposes of determining the quorum and the result of any
vote on a resolution under Condition 12 that relates to the removal or replacement of the Servicer, the waiver
of a Servicer default, or any other matter in which the Originator or its affiliates has, in the opinion of the
Trustee, an interest that conflicts with the interests of the other Noteholders. Notes held by or for the
Issuer or Holdings are not treated as outstanding for any of those purposes.

## Condition 3 — Security

**(a) The Security.** As continuing security for the payment of all amounts due on the Notes and to the other
Secured Creditors (the "Secured Obligations"), the Issuer grants to the Trustee, for itself and on trust for
the Secured Creditors, under the Deed of Charge:

(i) an assignment by way of first fixed security of its rights, title, interest and benefit in the
    Receivables, the Loan Agreements and the ancillary rights under them, including its beneficial interest
    in the Seller's title to the vehicles financed under the Loan Agreements (the "Financed Vehicles") and
    to the proceeds of their sale;
(ii) an assignment by way of first fixed security of its rights under each Transaction Document to which it
    is a party, other than the Trust Deed and the Deed of Charge themselves;
(iii) a first fixed charge over its rights in the Issuer Accounts and the amounts standing to their credit, and
    over any investments of those amounts in authorised investments (the "Authorised Investments"); and
(iv) a first floating charge over the whole of its undertaking, property and assets not otherwise effectively
    charged or assigned by way of fixed security, including its assets in Scotland,

(together, the "Security"). The floating charge is a qualifying floating charge for the purposes of paragraph 14
of Schedule B1 to the Insolvency Act 1986, and the Trustee may appoint an administrative receiver of the
Issuer in the circumstances permitted by section 72B of that Act (the capital market exception).

**(b) Perfection of the sale.** The sale of the Receivables to the Issuer takes effect in equity only. Notice of
the sale will not be given to the borrowers, and legal title to the Receivables and to the Financed Vehicles
will remain with the Seller, unless and until one of the perfection events set out in the Receivables Sale
Agreement occurs (each, a "Perfection Event"), which include the insolvency of the Seller, the termination of
the appointment of the Servicer, a direction from the Trustee following the delivery of an Enforcement Notice,
and a change of law requiring notice. After a Perfection Event the Issuer and the Trustee may give notice to
the borrowers and take the other steps described in section 12 to perfect the transfer. Until notice is given,
the risks described in section 2 in relation to equitable assignment and set-off apply.

**(c) Release of Security.** The Security over a Receivable is released automatically when it is repurchased by
the Seller under the Sale Agreement, when it is repaid in full or written off by the Servicer in
accordance with its credit and collection policies, or when the related Financed Vehicle is sold and the sale
proceeds are credited to the Transaction Account. The Security over the moneys in the Issuer Accounts is
released, without further action, to the extent that those moneys are applied in accordance with the
Pre-Enforcement Priority of Payments or as otherwise permitted by the Transaction Documents.

**(d) Enforceability.** The Security becomes enforceable on the delivery of an Enforcement Notice under
Condition 10. The Trustee may then take the steps described in Condition 11, and will apply all moneys received
by it in accordance with the Post-Enforcement Priority of Payments.

**(e) Trustee not responsible.** The Trustee will not be responsible for the adequacy, enforceability or
validity of the Security, for any failure to register or perfect the Security, or for monitoring the
performance of the Receivables, and it will have no liability for any loss arising from the Security being
held in a Clearing System or by a custodian selected with reasonable care. The Trustee may rely, without
further enquiry, on any certificate or report of the Issuer, the Servicer or the Cash Manager, and on the
opinion of any professional adviser.

## Condition 4 — Issuer covenants

**(a) Restrictions.** So long as any Note remains outstanding, the Issuer will not, except as provided in or
contemplated by the Transaction Documents or with the prior written consent of the Trustee:

(i) engage in any business other than acquiring, holding and administering the Portfolio, issuing the Notes
    and entering into and performing the Transaction Documents;
(ii) incur any indebtedness for borrowed money, or give any guarantee or indemnity in respect of any
    indebtedness, other than under the Notes and the Transaction Documents;
(iii) create or permit to subsist any mortgage, charge, pledge, lien or other security interest over the whole
    or any part of its assets or undertaking, other than the Security;
(iv) sell, transfer, lend or otherwise dispose of any of its assets, other than as permitted by the
    Transaction Documents;
(v) pay any dividend or make any other distribution to its shareholders, other than out of the profit retained
    by it in accordance with the Pre-Enforcement Priority of Payments, or issue any further shares;
(vi) have any subsidiary, employee or premises;
(vii) consolidate or merge with any other person or convey or transfer its properties or assets substantially
    as an entirety to any other person, other than in accordance with Condition 15;
(viii) have an interest in any bank account other than the Issuer Accounts, unless that account is charged to
    the Trustee on terms acceptable to it;
(ix) agree to any amendment, variation or waiver of any Transaction Document, or release any party from its
    obligations under it, other than in accordance with Condition 12;
(x) become a member of a group for the purposes of value added tax, or a member of any group for the purposes
    of corporation tax other than a group of which it is the principal company; or
(xi) take any action which would cause it to cease to be a "securitisation company" within the meaning of the
    Taxation of Securitisation Companies Regulations 2006 (SI 2006/3296).

**(b) Positive covenants.** The Issuer will at all times: (i) maintain its centre of main interests and its
registered office in England and Wales and conduct its business in its own name; (ii) keep proper books of
account and have its annual financial statements audited by Fennick & Rowe LLP or other independent auditors
appointed in accordance with the Companies Act 2006; (iii) hold at least one meeting of its board of
directors in the United Kingdom in each year and ensure that its affairs are managed and controlled in the
United Kingdom; (iv) deliver to the Trustee, within 180 days after the end of each financial year, a copy of
its audited financial statements and a certificate signed by two directors confirming that no Event of Default
has occurred, or giving details of any that has; (v) give notice to the Trustee promptly on becoming aware of
any Event of Default, Trigger Event or Perfection Event; (vi) maintain the Agents and the other service
providers required by the Transaction Documents; and (vii) do all such things as are necessary to maintain the
Security and to enable the Trustee to exercise its rights under it.

**(c) Securitisation Regulations.** The Issuer, as securitisation special purpose entity, and the Originator,
as originator, have agreed in the Transaction Documents to comply with the obligations that apply to them under
the Securitisation Regulations 2024 (SI 2024/102) and the related rules of the FCA and the PRA (together, the
"UK Securitisation Framework"). The Originator is designated as the entity responsible for making available the
information required by the transparency rules of the UK Securitisation Framework (the "Reporting Entity"), and
will make available each monthly investor report within 15 days after each Payment Date, together with the
loan-level data, as described in section 18. The Originator has undertaken to retain its interest as described
in Condition 2(f) and not to sell, hedge or otherwise mitigate the credit risk of the Retained Notes, save as
permitted by the UK Securitisation Framework.

**(d) Servicer and Cash Manager.** The Issuer will procure that, at all times while any Note is outstanding, a
servicer and a cash manager are appointed under the Servicing Agreement and the Cash Management Agreement. The
Issuer may terminate the appointment of the Servicer, and will do so if so directed by the Trustee, on the
occurrence of a servicer termination event set out in the Servicing Agreement (each, a "Servicer Termination
Event"), as described in section 13. No termination of the appointment of the Servicer takes effect until a
substitute servicer has been appointed on terms approved by the Trustee. The Back-up Servicer Facilitator will
assist the Issuer in identifying and appointing a substitute servicer in accordance with the Back-up Servicer
Facilitator Agreement. The Issuer will give notice to the Noteholders of any change of the Servicer or the Cash
Manager in accordance with Condition 14.

**(e) Account Bank.** If the Account Bank ceases to have the minimum ratings required by the Issuer
Accounts Agreement (as described in section 14), the Issuer will, within 30 calendar days, either transfer the
Issuer Accounts to a bank that has those ratings, or obtain a guarantee of the Account Bank's obligations
from a person that has those ratings, or take any other action in respect of which Rating Agency Confirmation
is obtained.

**(f) Further issues.** The Issuer will not issue any further notes, whether or not ranking *pari passu* with or
subordinated to any Class of Notes, and will not acquire any further receivables after the Closing Date, other
than replacement receivables substituted by the Seller in the limited circumstances permitted by the Receivables
Sale Agreement and described in section 12.

## Condition 5 — Interest

**(a) Accrual of interest.** Each Note bears interest on its Principal Amount Outstanding from (and including)
the Closing Date. Interest is payable monthly in arrear on each Payment Date in respect of the Interest Period
ending on (but excluding) that Payment Date. Each Note (or, in the case of the redemption of part only of a
Note, that part) ceases to bear interest from its due date for redemption, unless payment of principal is
improperly withheld or refused, in which case it continues to bear interest in accordance with this Condition
(both before and after judgment) until whichever is the earlier of: (i) the day on which all sums due on that
Note up to that day are received by or on behalf of the relevant Noteholder; and (ii) the day falling five
Business Days after the Principal Paying Agent or the Trustee has notified the Noteholders, in accordance with
Condition 14, that it has received all sums due on that Note up to that fifth Business Day (except to the
extent that there is any subsequent default in payment).

**(b) Rate of Interest.** The rate of interest payable on each Class of Notes for each Interest Period (the
"Rate of Interest") is the sum of Compounded Daily SONIA for that Interest Period, determined by the Agent Bank
under Condition 5(c), and the margin for that Class set out in section 4 (the "Relevant Margin"). The Relevant
Margin of each Class is fixed on the Closing Date for the life of the Notes and does not step up. If the Rate of
Interest so determined for any Class and any Interest Period is less than zero, the Rate of Interest for that
Class and that Interest Period will be zero.

**(c) Compounded Daily SONIA.** "Compounded Daily SONIA" means, for an Interest Period, the rate of return of a
daily compound interest investment in sterling (with the daily Sterling Overnight Index Average as the
reference rate for the calculation of interest), calculated by the Agent Bank on the Interest Determination
Date for that Interest Period as follows, the resulting percentage being rounded, if necessary, to the fourth
decimal place, with 0.00005 being rounded upwards:

(i) for each London Banking Day in the Interest Period, the Agent Bank takes the SONIA rate for the London
    Banking Day falling five London Banking Days before it (the "Lookback Rate"), multiplies it by the number
    of calendar days from (and including) that London Banking Day to (but excluding) the next London Banking
    Day, divides the result by 365, and adds one;
(ii) the Agent Bank then multiplies together all the factors obtained under paragraph (i) for the London
    Banking Days in the Interest Period, and subtracts one from the product; and
(iii) the Agent Bank multiplies the result by 365 and divides it by the number of calendar days in the
    Interest Period.

In this Condition: "**SONIA**" means, for any London Banking Day, a reference rate equal to the daily Sterling
Overnight Index Average rate for that London Banking Day as provided by the Bank of England, as administrator
of that rate (or any successor administrator), to authorised distributors and as then published on the
relevant screen page designated by those distributors (the "Relevant Screen Page") or, if the Relevant Screen
Page is unavailable, as otherwise published by those authorised distributors, in each case on the London
Banking Day immediately following that London Banking Day; "**London Banking Day**" means any day on which
commercial banks are open for general business (including dealing in foreign exchange and foreign currency
deposits) in London; and "**Interest Determination Date**" means, for each Interest Period, the fifth London
Banking Day before the Payment Date on which that Interest Period ends.

The lookback of five London Banking Days is applied to the SONIA rate only: the days on which the rate is
weighted and the number of days in the Interest Period are those of the Interest Period itself. The
"Compounded Daily SONIA" so determined for each Interest Period is reported in the monthly investor report as
the SONIA rate for the related Collection Period.

**(d) SONIA fallback.** If, in respect of any London Banking Day, the Agent Bank determines that SONIA is not
made available on the Relevant Screen Page and has not otherwise been published by the authorised
distributors, SONIA for that London Banking Day will be: (i) the Bank of England's Bank Rate (the "Bank Rate")
prevailing at close of business on that London Banking Day; plus (ii) the mean of the spread of SONIA to the
Bank Rate over the previous five days on which SONIA has been published, excluding the highest spread (or, if
there is more than one highest spread, one only of those highest spreads) and the lowest spread (or, if there
is more than one lowest spread, one only of those lowest spreads). Notwithstanding the preceding sentence, if
the Bank of England publishes guidance as to how SONIA is to be determined, or as to any rate that is to
replace SONIA, the Agent Bank will, to the extent that it is reasonably practicable, follow that guidance in
order to determine SONIA for so long as SONIA is not available or has not been published by the authorised
distributors.

If the Rate of Interest cannot be determined in accordance with Conditions 5(c) and 5(d) by the Agent Bank,
the Rate of Interest for the relevant Interest Period will be the Rate of Interest last determined for the
relevant Class for the immediately preceding Interest Period (or, if there has been no preceding Interest
Period, the rate which would have applied to the first Interest Period had the Notes been in issue for a full
month ending on the Closing Date). The application of this paragraph is without prejudice to the operation of
Condition 12(g) on the occurrence of a Benchmark Rate Disruption Event.

**(e) Interest Amount.** The Agent Bank will, as soon as practicable after the Interest Determination Date for
each Interest Period, calculate the amount of interest payable on each Class of Notes for that Interest Period
(the "Interest Amount"). The Interest Amount of a Class is the Principal Amount Outstanding of that Class on the
first day of the Interest Period (after taking into account any payment of principal made on that day)
multiplied by one-twelfth of the Rate of Interest of that Class, the result being rounded to the nearest penny
(half a penny being rounded upwards). The same fraction of one-twelfth applies to every Interest Period,
including the first Interest Period and any Interest Period that is lengthened or shortened because a Payment
Date is not a Business Day. The Interest Amount payable on each Note of a Class is the Interest Amount of that
Class multiplied by the fraction that the Principal Amount Outstanding of that Note represents of the Principal
Amount Outstanding of the Class, rounded down to the nearest penny.

**(f) Publication.** The Agent Bank will cause the Rate of Interest and the Interest Amount of each Class for
each Interest Period, and the related Payment Date, to be notified to the Issuer, the Cash Manager, the
Trustee, the Principal Paying Agent and the Clearing Systems as soon as possible after their determination
and in any event no later than the third Business Day before the related Payment Date, and will cause them to
be published in accordance with Condition 14. The Interest Amounts and the Payment Date so notified may be
amended (or appropriate alternative arrangements made by way of adjustment) without notice if the Interest
Period is extended or shortened. The Servicer will include the Rate of Interest and the Interest Amount of
each Class in the monthly investor report described in section 18.

**(g) Determination by the Trustee.** If the Agent Bank does not at any time determine the Rate of Interest or
calculate the Interest Amount of any Class in accordance with this Condition, the Trustee may (but is not
obliged to) do so, or appoint an agent to do so on its behalf, at the cost of the Issuer, in the manner
provided in this Condition, having regard as it thinks fit to the procedures described above. Each such
determination or calculation will be deemed to have been made by the Agent Bank, and the Trustee will have no
liability to any person in connection with it.

**(h) Notifications to be final.** All notifications, opinions, determinations, certificates, calculations,
quotations and decisions given, expressed, made or obtained for the purposes of this Condition, whether by the
Agent Bank or the Trustee, will (in the absence of wilful default, fraud or manifest error) be binding on the
Issuer, the Agent Bank, the Trustee, the other Agents and all Noteholders, and (in the absence of wilful
default, fraud or manifest error) no liability to the Issuer or the Noteholders will attach to the Agent Bank
or the Trustee in connection with the exercise or non-exercise by them of their powers, duties and discretions.

**(i) Interest deferral.** To the extent that, on any Payment Date before the delivery of an Enforcement Notice,
the Available Funds (including any amount drawn from the Reserve Fund in accordance with section 16) are not
sufficient, after paying the items ranking in priority to it in the Pre-Enforcement Priority of Payments, to
pay in full the Interest Amount of any Class other than the Most Senior Class, the amount of the shortfall
(the "Deferred Interest") will not then fall due. The Issuer will instead create a provision in its accounts
for the Deferred Interest and pay it, together with interest accrued on it at the Rate of Interest of the
relevant Class for each subsequent Interest Period, on the next Payment Date and on each later Payment Date on
which there are sufficient Available Funds to do so, in the position in the Pre-Enforcement Priority of
Payments of the interest of that Class. The deferral of interest under this paragraph is not an Event of
Default. All Deferred Interest and interest on it will in any event fall due on the earliest of the
redemption of the relevant Notes in full under Condition 6, the Final Maturity Date and the delivery of an
Enforcement Notice. Interest on the Most Senior Class may not be deferred: a failure to pay it in full when
due is an Event of Default under Condition 10(a)(i). The Cash Manager will notify the Noteholders, in
accordance with Condition 14, of any Deferred Interest on a Class.

**(j) The Agent Bank.** The Issuer will procure that, so long as any Note is outstanding, there is at all times
an Agent Bank. The Issuer may, with the prior written approval of the Trustee, terminate the appointment of
the Agent Bank and appoint another leading bank or financial institution in London to act in its place. If the
Agent Bank is unable or unwilling to continue to act, or fails duly to determine the Rate of Interest or
calculate the Interest Amount of any Class for any Interest Period, the Issuer will appoint a successor. The
Agent Bank may not resign its duties without a successor having been appointed.

## Condition 6 — Redemption

**(a) Final redemption.** Unless previously redeemed in full and cancelled as provided in this Condition, the
Issuer will redeem the Notes of each Class at their Principal Amount Outstanding, together with accrued
interest, on the Payment Date falling on 20 December 2032 (the "Final Maturity Date"). The Issuer may not
redeem or purchase Notes other than in accordance with this Condition. The Final Maturity Date falls after the
latest scheduled maturity of any Loan Agreement in the Portfolio, to allow time for the collection of balloon
payments, the recovery of amounts due on defaulted Loan Agreements and the sale of the related Financed
Vehicles.

**(b) Mandatory redemption in part.** On each Payment Date before the delivery of an Enforcement Notice, the
Issuer will redeem the Notes in part by applying, in accordance with the Pre-Enforcement Priority of Payments,
the amount of Available Funds remaining after the payment of the items ranking in priority to principal on the
Notes (the "Principal Payment Amount"). The Principal Payment Amount for each Payment Date is limited to the
amount needed to reduce the aggregate Principal Amount Outstanding of the Notes to the Pool Balance at the end
of the immediately preceding Collection Period (the "Target Principal Amount"). The Principal Payment Amount is
applied:

(i) until the first Payment Date following the occurrence of a Trigger Event, *pro rata* to the Principal
    Amount Outstanding of the Class A Notes, the Class B Notes, the Class C Notes and the Class D Notes; and
(ii) from (and including) the first Payment Date following the occurrence of a Trigger Event, sequentially:
    first to the Class A Notes until they are redeemed in full, then to the Class B Notes until they are
    redeemed in full, then to the Class C Notes until they are redeemed in full and finally to the Class D
    Notes, as described in section 4 and section 16.

The change from *pro rata* to sequential redemption under paragraph (ii) is permanent and does not reverse if
the Trigger Event ceases to apply. The amount applied to each Class is apportioned among the Notes of that
Class *pro rata* to their Principal Amount Outstanding, rounded down to the nearest penny.

**(c) Principal deficiency.** If the Principal Payment Amount for any Payment Date is less than the amount needed
to reduce the aggregate Principal Amount Outstanding of the Notes to the Target Principal Amount, the excess of
the aggregate Principal Amount Outstanding of the Notes (after the payments made on that Payment Date) over the
Pool Balance at the end of the preceding Collection Period is a "Principal Deficiency". A Principal Deficiency
is not an Event of Default. It is reduced on later Payment Dates by the application of Available Funds in the
manner described in section 15 and, to the extent not so reduced, it is borne by the Notes in reverse order of
seniority on the Final Maturity Date or on enforcement. The Cash Manager will record the amount of any
Principal Deficiency on each Calculation Date and the Servicer will report it in the monthly investor report.

**(d) Optional redemption: clean-up call.** On any Payment Date on which the Pool Balance at the end of the
immediately preceding Collection Period is less than 10% of the Pool Balance at the Cut-off Date, Kestrel Motor
Finance Ltd, as Seller, may under the Sale Agreement repurchase all (but not some) of the Loan
Agreements then held by the Issuer, together with all the related Receivables and the Issuer's interest in the
related Financed Vehicles (the "Clean-Up Call"). The repurchase price paid by the Seller funds the redemption of
the Notes. If the Seller exercises the Clean-Up Call, the Issuer will redeem all (but not some) of the Notes on that
Payment Date at their Principal Amount Outstanding together with accrued and unpaid interest (including any
Deferred Interest and interest on it), provided that:

(i) the Issuer has given not more than 60 nor fewer than 30 days' notice to the Trustee and to the Noteholders
    in accordance with Condition 14;
(ii) the repurchase price payable by the Seller is not less than the amount needed to redeem all the Notes as
    described above and to pay all amounts ranking in priority to or *pari passu* with the Notes in the
    Post-Enforcement Priority of Payments; and
(iii) before giving the notice, the Issuer has certified to the Trustee that it will have the necessary funds
    on the relevant Payment Date, and the Trustee is entitled to rely on that certificate without further
    enquiry.

The repurchase price for a defaulted Receivable is its fair market value, determined by the Servicer in
accordance with its credit and collection policies; for every other Receivable it is the outstanding principal
balance plus accrued interest. The Clean-Up Call is the only optional redemption of the Notes apart from
the tax call in Condition 6(e): the Notes do not have a step-up date or a call on a fixed date.

**(e) Optional redemption for tax reasons.** If the Issuer satisfies the Trustee immediately before giving the
notice referred to below that:

(i) on the next Payment Date the Issuer or any Paying Agent would be required to make a deduction or
    withholding for or on account of any present or future taxes, duties or charges of whatever nature from any
    payment of principal or interest on any Class of Notes (other than because a Noteholder has some connection
    with the United Kingdom other than the holding of the Notes), by reason of a change in the tax law of the
    United Kingdom (or of any political sub-division or authority of it) or in its application or official
    interpretation, which change becomes effective after the Closing Date; or
(ii) by reason of such a change, the Issuer would be subject to United Kingdom corporation tax in an accounting
    period on an amount which materially exceeds the amount of its retained profit for that period as
    described in section 19, or the borrowers would be required to make any deduction or withholding from a
    payment on the Receivables,

and that the Issuer has used reasonable endeavours (if necessary with the agreement of the Trustee) to avoid
that event by arranging the substitution of a company incorporated in another jurisdiction as principal
debtor under Condition 15, then the Issuer may, on any Payment Date and on giving not more than 60 nor fewer
than 30 days' notice to the Trustee and the Noteholders, redeem all (but not some) of the Notes at their
Principal Amount Outstanding together with accrued and unpaid interest (including any Deferred Interest and
interest on it). Before giving such notice the Issuer must deliver to the Trustee a certificate signed by two
directors stating that the relevant event has occurred and that it will have the funds, not subject to any
interest of any other person, needed to redeem the Notes and to pay all amounts ranking in priority to or
*pari passu* with them, and an opinion of independent legal advisers of recognised standing that the event has
occurred. The Trustee may accept the certificate and the opinion as sufficient evidence, and they will be
conclusive and binding on the Noteholders. The funds needed for the redemption will come from the sale of the
Receivables to the Seller or to a third party at a price not less than the amount described in Condition
6(d)(ii).

**(f) Principal Amount Outstanding and pool factor.** On each Calculation Date the Cash Manager will determine,
for each Class: (i) the amount of principal to be paid on that Class on the next Payment Date; (ii) the
Principal Amount Outstanding of each Note of that Class on that Payment Date after that payment; and (iii) the
fraction expressed as a decimal to the sixth decimal place (the "Pool Factor") of which the numerator is that
Principal Amount Outstanding and the denominator is the principal amount of that Note on the Closing Date. The
Cash Manager will notify those determinations to the Issuer, the Trustee, the Agents and the Clearing Systems no
later than the third Business Day before the Payment Date, and they will be published in accordance with
Condition 14. If the Cash Manager does not make any such determination, it will be made by the Trustee (or an
agent it appoints) in accordance with this Condition, and will be deemed to have been made by the Cash Manager.

**(g) Notice irrevocable.** Any notice of redemption given under Condition 6(d) or 6(e) is irrevocable, and on
its expiry the Issuer is bound to redeem the Notes concerned. A certificate given by the Issuer under those
Conditions binds the Noteholders.

**(h) Purchases and cancellation.** The Issuer may not purchase any Notes. All Notes redeemed in full will be
cancelled immediately and may not be reissued or resold. A Note that is redeemed in part will remain outstanding
for its reduced Principal Amount Outstanding.

**(i) No other redemption.** Except as described in this Condition and in Condition 11, the Notes will not be
redeemed before the Final Maturity Date. Noteholders have no right to require the Issuer to redeem their Notes.
The timing of redemption under Condition 6(b) depends on the rate at which borrowers repay, prepay or default on
the Receivables, on the exercise of voluntary termination rights under sections 99 and 100 of the Consumer
Credit Act 1974 and on the payment of balloon amounts under personal contract purchase agreements; the
weighted average lives of the Notes under various assumptions are described in section 17.

## Condition 7 — Payments

**(a) Method of payment.** Payments of principal and interest on the Notes will be made by transfer to the
registered account of the Noteholder. While the Notes are represented by a Global Note, each payment will be
made to, or to the order of, the holder of the Global Note, which will be the nominee of the common depositary
of the Clearing Systems, and each Accountholder must look solely to the relevant Clearing System for its share
of that payment. Payments on Definitive Notes will be made by transfer to a sterling account maintained by the
payee with a bank in London, details of which appear on the Register at the close of business on the fifteenth
day before the due date for payment (the "Record Date"). Payments of principal on the final redemption of a
Definitive Note will be made only against surrender of that Note at the specified office of the Registrar or of
any Paying Agent.

**(b) Payments subject to law.** All payments are subject in all cases to any applicable fiscal or other laws,
regulations and directives in the place of payment, and to any deduction or withholding required by an
agreement described in section 1471(b) of the US Internal Revenue Code of 1986 or otherwise imposed under
sections 1471 to 1474 of that Code, any regulations or agreements under them, any official interpretation of
them or any law implementing an intergovernmental approach to them, but without prejudice to Condition 8. No
commission or expense will be charged to the Noteholders in respect of any payment.

**(c) Payments on Business Days.** If the due date for any payment on a Note is not a Business Day, the holder is
not entitled to payment until the next Business Day and is not entitled to any further interest or other
payment as a result of the delay. The Interest Amount for an Interest Period is not adjusted for the delay, and
the next Interest Period ends on the next Payment Date determined in accordance with the definition of Payment
Date.

**(d) Partial payments.** If the amount of principal or interest paid on a Note is less than the amount then due
(including where interest is deferred under Condition 5(i)), the Registrar will annotate the Register with a
record of the amount paid, and that annotation is *prima facie* evidence that the payment has been made.

**(e) Paying Agents.** The initial Principal Paying Agent, Agent Bank and Registrar and their initial specified
offices are set out in section 21. The Issuer may, with the prior written approval of the
Trustee, vary or terminate the appointment of any Agent and appoint additional or other Agents, provided that it
will at all times maintain a Principal Paying Agent, an Agent Bank and a Registrar, each having a specified
office in London. Notice of any change in the Agents or their specified offices will be given to the
Noteholders in accordance with Condition 14. The Agents act solely as agents of the Issuer and, in certain
circumstances described in the Agency Agreement, of the Trustee, and do not assume any obligation or
relationship of agency or trust with or for any Noteholder.

**(f) Payments from the Issuer Accounts.** On the Business Day before each Payment Date, the Cash Manager will
instruct the Account Bank to transfer from the Transaction Account to the Principal Paying Agent the
amounts to be paid on the Notes on that Payment Date in accordance with the Pre-Enforcement Priority of Payments.
Each payment made by the Issuer to the Principal Paying Agent in accordance with the Agency Agreement discharges
the obligation of the Issuer to make the corresponding payment on the Notes, except to the extent that the
Principal Paying Agent subsequently fails to make that payment to the Noteholders.

## Condition 8 — Taxation

**(a) Payments without withholding.** All payments of principal and interest on the Notes will be made without
withholding or deduction for or on account of any present or future taxes, duties, assessments or governmental
charges of whatever nature, unless the Issuer, the Trustee or any Paying Agent (as the case may be) is required
by law to make such a withholding or deduction. In that event, the Issuer, the Trustee or the Paying Agent will
make the payment after the withholding or deduction and will account to the relevant authority for the amount
withheld or deducted.

**(b) No gross-up.** Neither the Issuer, nor the Trustee, nor any Paying Agent will be obliged to pay any
additional amount to any Noteholder in respect of any withholding or deduction made under Condition 8(a). The
imposition of such a withholding or deduction is not an Event of Default; it may, however, entitle the Issuer
to redeem the Notes under Condition 6(e).

**(c) United Kingdom withholding.** Because the Notes are not listed on a recognised stock exchange, the
exemption from United Kingdom withholding tax for quoted Eurobonds in section 882 of the Income Tax Act 2007 is
not available, and interest on the Notes is in principle payable after deduction of United Kingdom income tax at
the basic rate. The Issuer may nevertheless pay interest without withholding or deduction where an exemption or
relief applies, including where the Issuer reasonably believes that the person beneficially entitled to the
interest is a company within the charge to United Kingdom corporation tax in respect of it or is another person
listed in sections 933 to 937 of the Income Tax Act 2007, where the Notes are a qualifying private placement
within section 888A of that Act and the conditions for that exemption are met, or where HMRC has directed the
Issuer to pay interest without deduction (or at a reduced rate) under an applicable double taxation treaty. The
position, including the information Noteholders must provide in order to benefit from an exemption, is described
in section 19. Noteholders should take their own advice on the tax consequences of acquiring, holding and
disposing of the Notes.

**(d) Tax information.** Each Noteholder, by acquiring a Note or an interest in a Note, agrees to provide the
Issuer, the Paying Agents and the Trustee with any information, documentation or certification that they
reasonably request in order to determine whether a payment on the Notes may be made without, or at a reduced
rate of, withholding or deduction for or on account of tax, or in order to comply with any reporting
obligation of the Issuer under the law of the United Kingdom or under any intergovernmental agreement to which
the United Kingdom is a party.

## Condition 9 — Prescription

Claims against the Issuer for payment of principal on the Notes become void unless made within ten years, and
claims for payment of interest on the Notes become void unless made within five years, in each case from the
Relevant Date. In this Condition, the "**Relevant Date**" means, for any payment, the date on which it first
becomes due or (if the full amount payable has not been received by the Principal Paying Agent or the Trustee
on or before that date) the date on which, the full amount having been so received, notice to that effect is
given to the Noteholders in accordance with Condition 14. Any moneys paid by the Issuer to the Principal Paying
Agent for a payment that becomes void under this Condition will be repaid to the Issuer, and the Principal
Paying Agent will have no further liability for them. The periods in this Condition are without prejudice to
the Limitation Act 1980, which applies to any claim under the Trust Deed.

## Condition 10 — Events of default

**(a) Events of Default.** Each of the following events is an "Event of Default":

(i) the Issuer fails to pay any interest due on the Most Senior Class within five Business Days of the due date
    for that payment, or fails to pay any principal due on any Class of Notes within seven Business Days of
    the due date for that payment (it being understood that the deferral of interest under Condition 5(i), and
    any Principal Deficiency under Condition 6(c), is not a failure to pay an amount then due);
(ii) the Issuer fails to perform or observe any of its other obligations under the Notes, the Trust Deed, the
    Deed of Charge or any other Transaction Document, and (except where the Trustee considers that the failure
    is incapable of remedy, in which case no notice or continuation is required) the failure continues for 30
    days (or any longer period the Trustee permits) after the Trustee has given written notice of it to the
    Issuer requiring it to be remedied;
(iii) any representation or warranty made by the Issuer in any Transaction Document, or in any certificate
    delivered under it, proves to have been incorrect in any material respect when made or repeated, and the
    matters giving rise to the misrepresentation are not remedied within 30 days after the Trustee has given
    written notice of it to the Issuer;
(iv) an order is made or an effective resolution is passed for the winding up of the Issuer, except a winding
    up for the purposes of a reconstruction or amalgamation on terms previously approved in writing by the
    Trustee or by an Extraordinary Resolution of the Most Senior Class;
(v) the Issuer ceases or threatens to cease to carry on all or substantially all of its business, other than as
    permitted by the Transaction Documents, or the Issuer stops or threatens to stop payment of, or is unable
    or admits its inability to pay, its debts as they fall due, or is deemed unable to pay its debts within the
    meaning of section 123(1) or (2) of the Insolvency Act 1986 (on the basis that the reference in section
    123(1)(a) to £750 is to £1,000,000 or any higher amount the Trustee approves);
(vi) proceedings are initiated against the Issuer under any applicable liquidation, insolvency, composition,
    reorganisation or similar law (including the presentation of a petition for an administration order, the
    filing of documents with the court for the appointment of an administrator, or the giving of notice of
    intention to appoint an administrator, in each case other than by the Trustee), or an administrative or
    other receiver, administrator or similar official is appointed in relation to the Issuer or the whole or
    any substantial part of its undertaking or assets, or an encumbrancer takes possession of the whole or any
    substantial part of its undertaking or assets, or a distress, execution, diligence or other process is
    levied or enforced on the whole or any substantial part of its undertaking or assets and is not discharged
    within 14 days;
(vii) the Issuer initiates or consents to judicial proceedings relating to itself under any applicable
    liquidation, insolvency, composition, reorganisation or similar law, or makes a conveyance or assignment
    for the benefit of its creditors generally, or enters into any composition or other arrangement with its
    creditors generally (including a moratorium under Part A1 of the Insolvency Act 1986 or a restructuring
    plan under Part 26A of the Companies Act 2006); or
(viii) it is or becomes unlawful for the Issuer to perform or comply with any of its material obligations under
    the Notes or the Trust Deed.

The occurrence of a Trigger Event, a Servicer Termination Event or a Perfection Event is not, of itself, an
Event of Default.

**(b) Certification of material prejudice.** In the case of the events in Condition 10(a)(ii) and 10(a)(iii),
the Trustee may give an Enforcement Notice only if it has certified in writing to the Issuer that, in its
opinion, the event is materially prejudicial to the interests of the holders of the Most Senior Class. In
giving that certificate the Trustee may rely on the advice of any adviser it appoints and on any report of the
Servicer or the Cash Manager.

**(c) Delivery of an Enforcement Notice.** If an Event of Default occurs and is continuing, the Trustee at its
absolute discretion may, and shall if so requested in writing by the holders of at least 25% of the aggregate
Principal Amount Outstanding of the Most Senior Class or if so directed by an Extraordinary Resolution of the
holders of the Most Senior Class (subject in each case to its being indemnified and/or secured and/or
prefunded to its satisfaction), give written notice to the Issuer (an "Enforcement Notice") declaring the Notes
to be immediately due and repayable. On the delivery of an Enforcement Notice, all the Notes become immediately
due and repayable at their Principal Amount Outstanding together with accrued interest (including any Deferred
Interest and interest on it) as provided in the Trust Deed, and the Security becomes enforceable. Only the
holders of the Most Senior Class may direct the Trustee to deliver an Enforcement Notice; the holders of any
other Class have no right to do so while the Most Senior Class is outstanding.

**(d) Notice.** The Issuer will notify the Noteholders, the Rating Agencies and the other Secured Creditors of
the delivery of an Enforcement Notice in accordance with Condition 14 and the Transaction Documents. After the
delivery of an Enforcement Notice, no amount may be applied under the Pre-Enforcement Priority of Payments.

## Condition 11 — Enforcement and limited recourse

**(a) Proceedings.** At any time after the delivery of an Enforcement Notice, the Trustee may, at its discretion
and without further notice, take such proceedings, steps and actions against the Issuer or any other party to
any Transaction Document as it thinks fit to enforce the Security and the other provisions of the Notes, the
Trust Deed, the Deed of Charge and the other Transaction Documents, including the sale of the Receivables, the
appointment of a receiver or administrative receiver (a "Receiver"), and the giving of notice to the borrowers
under the Loan Agreements. The Trustee is not bound to take any such proceedings, steps or actions unless:

(i) it has been so directed by an Extraordinary Resolution of the holders of the Most Senior Class, or so
    requested in writing by the holders of at least 25% of the aggregate Principal Amount Outstanding of the
    Most Senior Class; and
(ii) it has been indemnified and/or secured and/or prefunded to its satisfaction against all liabilities,
    costs and expenses to which it may thereby become liable or which it may incur.

**(b) Sale of the Receivables.** The Trustee may sell the Receivables after the delivery of an Enforcement
Notice only if: (i) the proceeds of sale would be sufficient to discharge in full all amounts due on the Notes
and all amounts ranking in priority to or *pari passu* with them in the Post-Enforcement Priority of Payments;
or (ii) the Trustee is of the opinion, which it may reach on the advice of a financial adviser selected by it
(and on which it may rely without further enquiry), that the cash flows expected to be received by the Issuer
will not, or are not likely to, be sufficient to discharge those amounts as they fall due; or (iii) the sale is
sanctioned by an Extraordinary Resolution of the holders of each Class of Notes then outstanding.

**(c) Post-Enforcement Priority of Payments.** After the delivery of an Enforcement Notice, the Trustee will
apply all moneys received by it or by any Receiver in respect of the Security, including the amount standing to
the credit of the Reserve Account, in the order set out in the Deed of Charge. Under that order, the costs,
fees and expenses of the Trustee and any Receiver, and the amounts due to the Servicer, the Cash Manager, the
Account Bank, the Agents and the Corporate Services Provider, are paid first; then interest and
principal on the Class A Notes, interest and principal on the Class B Notes, interest and principal on the
Class C Notes and interest and principal on the Class D Notes, in that order, with interest on each Class being
paid before principal on that Class and no amount being paid on a Class until all amounts due on each Class
ranking above it have been paid in full; and finally deferred consideration to the Seller. The order differs
from the Pre-Enforcement Priority of Payments described in section 15 in that principal on each Class is paid
immediately after interest on that Class, and there is no reserve fund replenishment and no *pro rata*
redemption.

**(d) Only the Trustee may enforce.** No Noteholder is entitled to proceed directly against the Issuer or any
other party to the Transaction Documents, or to take any action to enforce the Security, unless the Trustee,
having become bound to do so, fails to do so within a reasonable period and the failure is continuing. Even
then, a Noteholder may not: (i) take any step for the purpose of recovering any of the Secured Obligations
owed to it, or enforcing any rights arising out of the Transaction Documents, other than in the name of the
Trustee and in accordance with the Deed of Charge; (ii) initiate or join any person in initiating any
insolvency, winding-up, administration or similar proceeding in relation to the Issuer, until the date falling
two years and one day after the date on which all the Notes have been redeemed in full; or (iii) take any step
that would result in the Post-Enforcement Priority of Payments not being observed.

**(e) Limited recourse.** The Notes are limited recourse obligations of the Issuer. The Issuer's assets
available to meet its obligations on the Notes are limited to the Receivables, the Financed Vehicles to the
extent of the Issuer's interest in them, the Issuer Accounts and its rights under the Transaction Documents. If
the Trustee, having realised the Security in full, certifies that there are no further assets of the Issuer
available to pay any amount outstanding on the Notes of any Class (after paying all amounts ranking in
priority to or *pari passu* with them), the Noteholders of that Class will have no further claim against the
Issuer for that amount, and the obligation of the Issuer to pay it will be extinguished. The Notes are not
obligations of, and are not guaranteed by, Kestrel Group plc, the Seller, the Servicer, the Originator, the
Trustee, the Cash Manager, the Account Bank, the Agents, the Corporate Services Provider, the Lead
Manager, Holdings or any other person.

**(f) No liability of directors.** No recourse may be had for any amount due on the Notes against any officer,
member, director, employee, security holder or incorporator of the Issuer or of Holdings, or against their
successors or assigns, in their capacity as such.

**(g) Trustee's rights.** The Trust Deed contains provisions for the indemnification of the Trustee and for its
relief from responsibility, including provisions relieving it from taking proceedings to enforce repayment
unless indemnified and/or secured and/or prefunded to its satisfaction. The Trustee and its affiliates may enter
into business transactions with the Issuer, the Seller, the Servicer and any of their affiliates without
accounting for any profit. The Trustee is not liable for any loss arising from its acting on any instruction
given by an Extraordinary Resolution, or by the holders of the required percentage of the Most Senior Class,
even if it later appears that the instruction was not validly given.

## Condition 12 — Meetings of noteholders, modification and waiver

**(a) Meetings.** The Trust Deed contains provisions for convening meetings of the Noteholders of any Class, or
of two or more Classes, to consider matters affecting their interests, including the sanctioning by
Extraordinary Resolution of a modification of the Conditions or of any Transaction Document. A meeting may be
convened by the Issuer or by the Trustee, and the Trustee must convene a meeting if requested in writing by
Noteholders holding at least 10% of the aggregate Principal Amount Outstanding of the relevant Class or Classes,
subject to being indemnified and/or secured and/or prefunded to its satisfaction. At least 21 clear days'
notice (exclusive of the day on which the notice is given and of the day of the meeting) must be given of a
meeting. Meetings may be held in person, by electronic platform or by a combination of both, as the Trust Deed
provides.

**(b) Resolutions.** An "**Ordinary Resolution**" is a resolution passed at a quorate meeting by a majority of
more than 50% of the votes cast. An "**Extraordinary Resolution**" is a resolution passed at a quorate meeting by
a majority of at least 75% of the votes cast. A resolution in writing signed by or on behalf of the holders of
at least 75% (for an Extraordinary Resolution) or more than 50% (for an Ordinary Resolution) of the aggregate
Principal Amount Outstanding of the relevant Class (a "**Written Resolution**"), and consent given by electronic
means through the Clearing Systems by holders of the same proportion (an "**Electronic Consent**"), each takes
effect as if passed at a duly convened meeting.

**(c) Quorum.** The quorum at a meeting to pass an Ordinary Resolution is one or more persons holding or
representing more than 25% of the aggregate Principal Amount Outstanding of the relevant Class (or, at an
adjourned meeting, one or more persons holding or representing any amount). The quorum at a meeting to pass an
Extraordinary Resolution, other than one sanctioning a Basic Terms Modification, is one or more persons holding
or representing more than 50% of the aggregate Principal Amount Outstanding of the relevant Class (or, at an
adjourned meeting, more than 25%). The quorum at a meeting to pass an Extraordinary Resolution sanctioning a
Basic Terms Modification is one or more persons holding or representing at least 75% of the aggregate Principal
Amount Outstanding of the relevant Class (or, at an adjourned meeting, at least 25%).

**(d) Basic Terms Modifications.** A "**Basic Terms Modification**" is a modification that would: (i) change any
date fixed for payment of principal or interest on any Class, including the Final Maturity Date; (ii) reduce
the amount of principal or interest payable on any Class, or change the method of calculating it, other than a
Benchmark Rate Modification under Condition 12(g); (iii) change the currency of payment of any Class; (iv) alter
the Pre-Enforcement Priority of Payments or the Post-Enforcement Priority of Payments in a way that affects the
amount or timing of payments on any Class; (v) release the Security other than as permitted by the
Transaction Documents; (vi) change the quorum required at any meeting or the majority required to pass an
Extraordinary Resolution; or (vii) amend this definition. A Basic Terms Modification takes effect only if
sanctioned by an Extraordinary Resolution of the holders of each Class then outstanding.

**(e) Effect on other Classes.** An Extraordinary Resolution passed by the holders of the Most Senior Class is
binding on the holders of all the other Classes, irrespective of its effect on them, except an Extraordinary
Resolution sanctioning a Basic Terms Modification. No Extraordinary Resolution of the holders of any other Class
is effective unless the Trustee is of the opinion that it will not be materially prejudicial to the interests
of the holders of each Class ranking above it, or it is sanctioned by an Extraordinary Resolution of the holders
of each such Class. A resolution passed at a meeting of a Class is binding on all the holders of that Class,
whether or not they were present or voted.

**(f) Modification and waiver without the consent of Noteholders.** The Trustee may agree, without the consent or
sanction of the Noteholders or the other Secured Creditors, to: (i) any modification of the Conditions or of
any Transaction Document (other than a Basic Terms Modification) that, in the opinion of the Trustee, is not
materially prejudicial to the interests of the holders of the Most Senior Class; (ii) any modification that, in
its opinion, is of a formal, minor or technical nature or is made to correct a manifest error or an error which
is, to its satisfaction, proven; and (iii) any waiver or authorisation of any breach or proposed breach by the
Issuer of the Conditions or any Transaction Document, or any determination that an Event of Default is not to be
treated as such, if in its opinion the interests of the holders of the Most Senior Class will not be materially
prejudiced by it. The Trustee may not exercise its power under paragraph (iii) in contravention of any express
direction given by an Extraordinary Resolution of the holders of the Most Senior Class or of a written request
by the holders of at least 25% of the Principal Amount Outstanding of that Class.

In addition, the Trustee is obliged, without the consent of the Noteholders, to concur with the Issuer in making
any modification (other than a Basic Terms Modification) that the Issuer, or the Servicer on its behalf,
certifies in writing is required solely: (i) to comply with any change in the criteria of a Rating Agency that
applies to the Account Bank or to any other counterparty, or to enable the Issuer to appoint a
replacement counterparty; (ii) to comply with the UK Securitisation Framework, including any change to the
reporting or retention requirements, or with any obligation under the UK version of Regulation (EU) No 648/2012
on derivatives, central counterparties and trade repositories; (iii) to enable the Notes to be, or to remain,
eligible as collateral for any central bank facility; or (iv) to avoid the Issuer becoming liable to taxation
or ceasing to be a securitisation company, provided in each case that the Issuer has given at least 30 days'
notice to the Noteholders of the proposed modification and that holders of 10% or more of the aggregate
Principal Amount Outstanding of the Most Senior Class have not objected to it in writing within that period.
The Trustee is not obliged to concur in any modification that would, in its opinion, expose it to any
liability against which it has not been indemnified and/or secured and/or prefunded to its satisfaction, or
that would add to its obligations or reduce its protections.

**(g) Benchmark Rate Modification.** If a Benchmark Rate Disruption Event occurs, the Issuer (or the Servicer on
its behalf) may propose, and the Trustee will be obliged to concur with the Issuer in making, without the
consent of the Noteholders, a modification of the Conditions and the Transaction Documents to change the
benchmark rate on the Notes from SONIA to an alternative benchmark rate (an "Alternative Benchmark Rate") and to
make any consequential amendments, including to the Relevant Margin by way of an adjustment spread (a
"**Benchmark Rate Modification**"), provided that:

(i) the Issuer, or the Servicer on its behalf, certifies in writing to the Trustee (a "Benchmark Rate
    Modification Certificate") that a Benchmark Rate Disruption Event has occurred, that the modification is
    being made solely as a result of it and is required solely to implement the Alternative Benchmark Rate, and
    that the Alternative Benchmark Rate is: a rate formally recommended or published by the Bank of England,
    the FCA or the PRA or any working group or committee sponsored by any of them; or a rate that has replaced
    SONIA in customary market usage for sterling asset-backed floating rate notes; or such other rate as the
    Issuer reasonably determines, with reasons, to be appropriate;
(ii) the Issuer has given at least 30 days' notice of the proposed Benchmark Rate Modification to the
    Noteholders in accordance with Condition 14, and to the Trustee and the Rating Agencies; and
(iii) the holders of at least 10% of the aggregate Principal Amount Outstanding of the Most Senior Class have
    not notified the Issuer or the Principal Paying Agent in writing, within 30 days of the notice, that they
    do not consent to the modification.

If holders of 10% or more of the Most Senior Class object, the Benchmark Rate Modification will not be made
unless it is sanctioned by an Extraordinary Resolution of the holders of the Most Senior Class (for which
purpose the quorum and majority for an Extraordinary Resolution that is not a Basic Terms Modification apply).
A "**Benchmark Rate Disruption Event**" means any of: (i) a material disruption to SONIA, a material change in
its methodology or SONIA ceasing to exist or to be published; (ii) a public statement by the administrator of
SONIA that it has ceased or will cease to publish SONIA permanently or indefinitely (where no successor
administrator has been appointed); (iii) a public statement by the supervisor of the administrator of SONIA that
SONIA has been or will be permanently or indefinitely discontinued, prohibited from being used or is no longer
representative of its underlying market; (iv) it becoming unlawful for the Agent Bank, the Issuer or any Paying
Agent to calculate any payment due on the Notes using SONIA; or (v) the reasonable expectation of the Issuer
that any of the events in paragraphs (i) to (iv) will occur within six months. The Issuer may make a single
Benchmark Rate Modification for the Notes of all Classes at the same time, and the same Alternative Benchmark
Rate and the same method of determining it will apply to every Class. The Relevant Margin of each Class will
remain as set out in section 4, adjusted only by the adjustment spread forming part of the Benchmark Rate
Modification.

**(h) Notification.** Any modification, waiver, authorisation or determination under this Condition is binding on
all the Noteholders and on the other Secured Creditors and, unless the Trustee agrees otherwise, will be notified
by the Issuer to the Noteholders in accordance with Condition 14 and to the Rating Agencies as soon as practicable
after it has been made.

**(i) Trustee's considerations.** In determining whether a matter is materially prejudicial to the interests of
the holders of any Class, the Trustee may rely on a Rating Agency Confirmation, but is not bound to do so and a
Rating Agency Confirmation is not a substitute for its own judgement. A Rating Agency Confirmation addresses only
the effect of a matter on the ratings of the Notes and does not address its effect on the interests of the
Noteholders generally.

## Condition 13 — Replacement of notes

If a Definitive Note is mutilated, defaced, lost, stolen or destroyed, it may be replaced at the specified
office of the Registrar, subject to all applicable laws, on payment by the claimant of the expenses incurred in
connection with the replacement and on such terms as to evidence, security and indemnity as the Issuer and the
Registrar may reasonably require. A mutilated or defaced Definitive Note must be surrendered before a
replacement is issued. If a Definitive Note that has been replaced is subsequently presented for payment, the
claimant must pay to the Issuer on demand the amount the Issuer pays on that Note. A Global Note that is
mutilated, defaced, lost, stolen or destroyed will be replaced by the Issuer on the same terms, without any
expense to the Accountholders.

## Condition 14 — Notices

**(a) Notices while in global form.** While the Notes are represented by Global Notes, notices to the Noteholders
will be valid if delivered to the Clearing Systems for communication by them to the Accountholders, and will be
deemed to have been given on the day after the day on which they are delivered. Each investor report, and each
notice of the Rate of Interest, the Interest Amounts, the Principal Payment Amount and the Pool Factor, will
also be made available on the website maintained by the Originator as Reporting Entity for the purposes of the
UK Securitisation Framework, as described in section 18; the availability of a report on that website is not a
condition of the validity of a notice.

**(b) Notices while in definitive form.** If Definitive Notes are issued, notices to the Noteholders will be valid
if sent by first-class post (or, if posted to an address outside the United Kingdom, by airmail) to the holders
at their respective addresses shown in the Register, and will be deemed to have been given on the fourth day
after the date of posting.

**(c) Other methods.** The Trustee may approve any other method of giving notice to the Noteholders, or to the
holders of any Class, if in its opinion that method is reasonable having regard to the market practice then
prevailing and to the requirements of the Clearing Systems, and notice so given will be deemed to have been
given on the date the Trustee approves.

**(d) Notices from Noteholders.** Notices to be given by any Noteholder must be in writing and given by lodging
them, with evidence of the holding satisfactory to the Principal Paying Agent, with the Principal Paying Agent
or, while the Notes are represented by Global Notes, through the Clearing Systems in such manner as the
Principal Paying Agent and the Clearing Systems approve.

**(e) Rating Agencies.** The Issuer will give a copy of every notice given under this Condition to each Rating
Agency. A failure to do so does not affect the validity of the notice.

## Condition 15 — Substitution of the issuer

**(a) Substitution.** The Trustee may agree with the Issuer, without the consent of the Noteholders or the other
Secured Creditors, to the substitution of another company (the "Substituted Obligor") in place of the Issuer
as principal debtor under the Notes, the Trust Deed and the other Transaction Documents, provided that:

(i) a deed is executed, or some other form of undertaking is given, by the Substituted Obligor, in a form and
    manner satisfactory to the Trustee, agreeing to be bound by the Notes, the Trust Deed and the other
    Transaction Documents as if it had been named in them as principal debtor;
(ii) the Substituted Obligor acquires the Issuer's interest in the Receivables and its other assets, and grants
    security over them in favour of the Trustee equivalent to the Security;
(iii) the Substituted Obligor is a special purpose company, with restrictions on its business equivalent to
    those in Condition 4, and its directors certify that it will be solvent immediately after the
    substitution;
(iv) the Trustee is satisfied that the substitution will not be materially prejudicial to the interests of the
    holders of the Most Senior Class, and Rating Agency Confirmation has been obtained for the substitution;
(v) where the Substituted Obligor is incorporated, domiciled or resident for tax purposes in a territory other
    than the United Kingdom, undertakings or covenants are given in terms corresponding to Condition 8 and
    Condition 6(e), with references to that territory in addition to the United Kingdom, and legal opinions
    satisfactory to the Trustee are delivered as to the law of that territory; and
(vi) the substitution complies with the UK Securitisation Framework and does not cause the Originator to cease to
    comply with its retention obligation described in Condition 2(f).

**(b) Effect.** On the substitution taking effect, the Substituted Obligor will be treated as the Issuer for every
purpose, and the Issuer will be released from its obligations as principal debtor. The Issuer will give notice
of the substitution to the Noteholders in accordance with Condition 14 within 14 days of its taking effect. In
connection with any substitution, the Trustee will not have regard to the consequences for individual
Noteholders resulting from their being domiciled or resident in, or otherwise connected with, any particular
territory, and no Noteholder is entitled to claim from the Issuer, the Substituted Obligor or the Trustee any
indemnification or payment in respect of any tax consequence of the substitution for that Noteholder.

## Condition 16 — Rights of third parties

No person has any right under the Contracts (Rights of Third Parties) Act 1999 to enforce any term of the Notes,
but this does not affect any right or remedy of any person which exists or is available apart from that Act.
The parties to the Trust Deed may rescind or vary it without the consent of any person who is not a party to
it, subject to Condition 12.

## Condition 17 — Governing law and jurisdiction

**(a) Governing law.** The Notes, the Trust Deed, the Deed of Charge, the Agency Agreement and the other
Transaction Documents, and any non-contractual obligation arising out of or in connection with any of them, are
governed by and will be construed in accordance with English law.

**(b) Jurisdiction.** The courts of England have exclusive jurisdiction to settle any dispute arising out of or in
connection with the Notes, including any dispute as to their existence, validity, interpretation, performance,
breach or termination, or the consequences of their nullity, and any dispute relating to any non-contractual
obligation arising out of or in connection with them (a "Dispute"). The Issuer submits to the jurisdiction of
the courts of England and waives any objection to them on the grounds that they are an inconvenient or
inappropriate forum. This Condition is for the benefit of the Trustee and the Noteholders only. To the extent
permitted by law, the Trustee may, in respect of any Dispute, take proceedings in any other court with
jurisdiction, and concurrent proceedings in any number of jurisdictions.

**(c) Service of process.** The Issuer has its registered office in England and Wales and agrees that service of
process in any proceedings in England may be effected by delivery to that registered office, or to any other
address in England of which it gives notice to the Trustee.

\newpage

# 6. Use of proceeds

The gross proceeds of the issue of the Notes on the Closing Date will be equal to the aggregate initial
principal amount of the Notes set out in section 4, which is equal to the pool balance at the Cut-off Date
(£74,075,862.24). The Issuer will apply the whole of those proceeds on the Closing Date to pay to the Seller
the initial purchase price for the 5,000 loans in the portfolio and their related rights, under the Loan
Sale Agreement (the "Initial Purchase Price"). The Seller will apply part of the Initial Purchase Price to repay
the amounts outstanding under the private warehouse facility from which certain of the Loans are released on the
Closing Date, as described in section 8, and will receive the balance for its general corporate purposes.

In addition to the Initial Purchase Price, the Issuer will pay to the Seller deferred consideration for the
Loans (the "Deferred Consideration"). Deferred Consideration is payable on each Payment Date only out of the
Available Funds that remain after every higher-ranking item in the Priority of Payments has been paid in full,
as set out in section 15. The Seller has no claim against the Issuer for Deferred Consideration on any Payment
Date on which those funds are insufficient.

The reserve fund described in section 16 will be funded by the Originator on the Closing Date and does not
reduce the proceeds applied to the Initial Purchase Price. The fees and expenses of the Arranger and the Lead
Manager and the other costs incurred in connection with the issue of the Notes will be paid by the Originator
and will not be paid out of the proceeds of the Notes. None of the proceeds will be retained by the Issuer as
profit, and the Issuer will not use any part of them for any other purpose.

\newpage

# 7. The issuer and Holdings

## Incorporation and status

Thames Auto Receivables 2026-1 plc (the "Issuer") was incorporated in England and Wales on 4 November 2025 as a
public company limited by shares under the Companies Act 2006, with registered number 16048213. The registered
office of the Issuer is at 8 Aldermary Lane, London EC4, and that is also its principal place of business. The
Issuer has no telephone number of its own; it may be contacted through the Corporate Services Provider at the
same address.

The Issuer was established as a special purpose vehicle for the purpose of issuing asset-backed securities. It
is an orphan company: it is not a subsidiary of the Originator or of any company in the Kestrel group, and no
party to the Transaction Documents other than Holdings has any ownership interest in it. The Issuer is a
"securitisation company" for the purposes of the Taxation of Securitisation Companies Regulations 2006 (SI
2006/3296), as described in section 19.

## Share capital of the Issuer

The issued share capital of the Issuer is £50,000, divided into 50,000 ordinary shares of £1 each. One share is
fully paid up and 49,999 shares are each paid up as to 25 pence, so that £12,500.75 of the issued share
capital has been paid up. Holdings is the legal and beneficial owner of 49,999 shares, and Pellam Corporate
Services Ltd holds the remaining share as nominee for Holdings under a declaration of trust in favour of
Holdings. The Issuer has no other class of shares, no shares held in treasury and no outstanding options,
warrants or rights to subscribe for shares, and it has not issued any convertible or exchangeable securities.

The rights of the shareholders are set out in the articles of association of the Issuer. Holdings may not
dispose of, or create any security interest over, the shares in the Issuer except in accordance with the
Transaction Documents.

## Principal activities of the Issuer

The principal objects of the Issuer are set out in its articles of association and include the purchase of
receivables, the issue of debt securities, the entry into financial arrangements in connection with them and
the grant of security for its obligations. Since its incorporation the Issuer has not engaged in any material
activities other than those incidental to its incorporation, to its registration as a public company under the
Companies Act 2006, to the authorisation and issue of the Notes, to the matters described or contemplated in
this Prospectus and to the authorisation, execution, delivery and performance of the Transaction Documents. The
Issuer has not commenced operations and has not prepared any financial statements as at the date of this
Prospectus.

From the Closing Date the Issuer's activities will consist of holding the Loans and their related rights,
receiving collections on them through the Servicer, paying interest and principal on the Notes and its other
obligations in the order set out in the Priority of Payments in section 15, and performing its obligations and
exercising its rights under the Transaction Documents. All of its assets will be charged to the Trustee under
the Deed of Charge to secure its obligations to the Secured Creditors.

## Restrictions on the Issuer

So long as any Note is outstanding, the Issuer has covenanted with the Trustee in the Trust Deed that, save as
permitted by the Transaction Documents or with the prior written consent of the Trustee, it will not:

(a) carry on any business other than as described in this Prospectus and as contemplated by the Transaction
Documents;

(b) incur any indebtedness for borrowed money, or give any guarantee or indemnity in respect of the
indebtedness of any person, other than the Notes and the obligations it assumes under the Transaction
Documents;

(c) create or permit to subsist any mortgage, charge, pledge, lien or other security interest over any of its
assets or its undertaking, other than the security created by the Deed of Charge;

(d) sell, transfer or otherwise dispose of any of its assets, other than in accordance with the Transaction
Documents;

(e) pay any dividend or make any other distribution to its shareholders, or issue any further shares;

(f) have any employees, own or lease any premises, or have any subsidiary undertaking;

(g) consolidate or merge with any other person or convey or transfer all or substantially all of its
properties or assets to any other person;

(h) open or have an interest in any bank account other than the Issuer Accounts, unless the account is charged
to the Trustee under the Deed of Charge;

(i) amend, supplement or waive any provision of its articles of association or of any Transaction Document,
except as provided in the Conditions (section 5); or

(j) take any action which would cause it to cease to be a securitisation company for the purposes of the
Taxation of Securitisation Companies Regulations 2006, or become resident for tax purposes in any jurisdiction
other than the United Kingdom.

## Directors and company secretary

The directors of the Issuer and their business addresses and principal outside activities are set out in the
table below. Each director is an employee or officer of the Corporate Services Provider, which supplies the
directors to the Issuer under the Corporate Services Agreement.

| Name | Business address | Principal outside activities |
|---|---|---|
| Helena Ashcombe | 8 Aldermary Lane, London EC4 | Director of special purpose companies, Pellam Corporate Services Ltd |
| Rupert Vane-Dalby | 8 Aldermary Lane, London EC4 | Head of corporate administration, Pellam Corporate Services Ltd |
| Priya Sandeford | 8 Aldermary Lane, London EC4 | Director of special purpose companies, Pellam Corporate Services Ltd |

The company secretary of the Issuer is Pellam Corporate Services Ltd, whose registered office is at 8 Aldermary
Lane, London EC4.
