The directors of the Issuer are also the directors of Holdings and act as directors of other special purpose
companies, some of which may be parties to other securitisation transactions. There are no potential conflicts
of interest between the duties of the directors to the Issuer and their private interests or other duties,
save that the directors are employees or officers of the Corporate Services Provider, which receives a fee for
its services under the Corporate Services Agreement. The Corporate Services Agreement provides
that the directors supplied by the Corporate Services Provider will act in the interests of the Issuer and in
accordance with their duties under the Companies Act 2006, and not on the instructions of the Originator. The
Issuer has no non-executive directors who are independent of the Corporate Services Provider and has not
established an audit committee or a remuneration committee. As a company whose securities are not listed, the
Issuer is not required to comply with the UK Corporate Governance Code and does not do so.

The appointment of the Corporate Services Provider may be terminated by the Issuer, with the prior written
consent of the Trustee, on the occurrence of certain events, including the insolvency of the Corporate Services
Provider or its material breach of the Corporate Services Agreement. The Corporate Services Provider may resign
on not less than 90 days' notice, provided that a substitute corporate services provider has been appointed on
substantially the same terms.

## Capitalisation of the Issuer

The table below shows the capitalisation and indebtedness of the Issuer as at the Closing Date, adjusted for

## Holdings

Thames Auto Receivables Holdings Ltd ("Holdings") was incorporated in England and Wales on 3 November 2025 as a
private company limited by shares under the Companies Act 2006, with registered number 16047795. Its
registered office is at 8 Aldermary Lane, London EC4. The issued share capital of Holdings is £1, consisting of
one ordinary share of £1, which is fully paid up and held by the Share Trustee.

The principal objects of Holdings are to acquire and hold, by way of investment, the shares in the Issuer and
to carry on any business incidental to that purpose. Holdings has not engaged in any activities since its
incorporation other than those incidental to its incorporation, to the acquisition of the shares in the Issuer
and to the matters contemplated in this Prospectus. Holdings has no employees and no subsidiary other than the
Issuer, and it has given covenants to the Trustee, in a deed dated on or about the Closing Date, that it will
not dispose of or charge the shares of the Issuer, or permit the Issuer to issue further shares, while any
Note is outstanding. The directors of Holdings are the same persons as the directors of the Issuer, and its
company secretary is Pellam Corporate Services Ltd.

Holdings has no right to any payment from the Issuer under the Transaction Documents and receives no dividend
from the Issuer. It is not a party to the Deed of Charge and does not guarantee any obligation of the Issuer.

## The share trust

The Share Trustee holds the share in Holdings under the terms of the Share Trust Deed on a discretionary trust
for the benefit of one or more charities or for charitable purposes. The Share Trustee has no beneficial
interest in, and derives no benefit (other than its fees for acting as share trustee) from, its holding of the
share in Holdings. Any income it receives under the trust is applied for the benefit of the charitable
purposes in its discretion. The Share Trust Deed provides that the trust will be wound up, and the shares of
Holdings disposed of, only after all amounts due under the Notes and the other Transaction Documents have been
paid in full.

This structure is intended to ensure that neither the Issuer nor Holdings is a subsidiary undertaking of the
Originator or of any other party to the transaction for the purposes of the Companies Act 2006, and that the
assets of the Issuer are not consolidated with those of the Originator on the insolvency of the Originator. The
Originator does not control, directly or indirectly, the Issuer, Holdings or the Share Trustee, and none of
them is accustomed to act in accordance with the directions of the Originator.

## No employees and no subsidiaries

Neither the Issuer nor Holdings has, or will have while any Note is outstanding, any employees. The
administrative functions of the Issuer are performed by the Corporate Services Provider, the servicing of the
Loans by the Servicer and the operation of its bank accounts and the calculation of payments by the Cash
Manager, in each case under the relevant Transaction Document. The Issuer has no subsidiary undertakings and,
other than the Issuer, Holdings has none.

## Accounts and auditors

The accounting reference date of the Issuer and of Holdings is 31 December. The first statutory accounts of
the Issuer will be prepared for the period from its incorporation to 31 December 2026 and, in accordance with
the Companies Act 2006, will be filed at Companies House within six months of that date, as a public company.
The Issuer will prepare its accounts in accordance with United Kingdom generally accepted accounting practice,
including Financial Reporting Standard 102. Holdings will prepare its accounts in the same way and, as a
private company, will file them within nine months of its accounting reference date. Neither the Issuer nor
Holdings has prepared any financial statements or published any accounts as at the date of this Prospectus.

Fennick & Rowe LLP, chartered accountants and registered auditors, of London, have been appointed as the
auditors of the Issuer and of Holdings. Fennick & Rowe LLP is registered to carry out audit work in the United
Kingdom by the Institute of Chartered Accountants in England and Wales. The Auditors have no material interest
in the Issuer or in Holdings.

Copies of the audited annual accounts of the Issuer, when published, will be available for inspection as
described in section 21, and the Issuer will deliver a copy of them to the Trustee and to the Principal Paying
Agent. The Issuer is not required by the Conditions to prepare interim accounts.

# 8. The originator, seller and servicer

The information in this section has been provided by Kestrel Motor Finance Ltd and relates to Kestrel Motor
Finance Ltd and its group. The Issuer confirms that this information has been accurately reproduced and that,
so far as it is aware and is able to ascertain from information published or provided by Kestrel Motor Finance
Ltd, no facts have been omitted which would render the reproduced information inaccurate or misleading. None of
Corvane Securities plc (the "Arranger" and the "Lead Manager"), Larkmead Bank plc (the "Cash Manager"), the
Issuer or the Trustee has independently verified this information or accepts any responsibility for it, other than
the responsibility of the Issuer stated in this paragraph. Financial and operational figures in this section
describe the business of Kestrel Motor Finance Ltd as a whole; they are not figures for the Portfolio, which is
described only in section 10, and they are not terms of the Notes.

## Introduction

Kestrel Motor Finance Ltd ("Kestrel" and, in its respective capacities in the Transaction, the "Originator", the
"Seller" and the "Servicer") is a private limited company incorporated in England and Wales on 12 March 2004
under the Companies Act 1985 with registered number 05087341. Kestrel is a wholly owned subsidiary of Kestrel
Group plc (the "Parent" and, together with its subsidiaries, the "Kestrel Group"), a public limited company
incorporated in England and Wales which acts as the holding company of the Kestrel Group and has no operating
business of its own. The shares of the Parent are not admitted to trading on any market; they are held by the
founding shareholders, members of senior management under an employee share plan and a small number of
institutional investors, none of which holds a controlling interest.

Kestrel is authorised and regulated by the Financial Conduct Authority (the "FCA") under the Financial Services
and Markets Act 2000 ("FSMA") for the regulated activities of entering into regulated credit agreements as
lender, exercising or having the right to exercise the lender's rights and duties under regulated credit
agreements, credit broking, debt collecting and debt administration. Kestrel is entered on the Financial
Services Register maintained by the FCA. Kestrel is also registered with the Information Commissioner's Office
as a controller of personal data and is a member of the principal UK trade association for consumer and motor
finance providers, whose lending code it has adopted.

Kestrel's business consists of the provision of point-of-sale finance to individual consumers for the purchase
of new and used cars and light commercial vehicles supplied by franchised and independent motor dealers in
England, Wales, Scotland and Northern Ireland. Its two principal products are hire purchase agreements ("HP
Agreements") and personal contract purchase agreements ("PCP Agreements" and, together with HP Agreements, the
"Finance Agreements"), each of which is a fixed-sum credit agreement regulated by the Consumer Credit Act 1974
(the "CCA") under which title to the financed vehicle remains with Kestrel until the borrower has paid all
amounts due and exercised the option to purchase. Kestrel does not offer leasing products, does not lend on an
unsecured basis and does not lend to businesses other than sole traders and small partnerships whose agreements
are regulated by the CCA; agreements with sole traders and partnerships are not included in the Portfolio.

## History and development

Kestrel was founded in 2004 by a group of former managers of the motor finance division of a UK clearing bank,
with the intention of offering independent and medium-sized franchised dealers in the Midlands and the north of
England a responsive alternative to the captive finance companies of the vehicle manufacturers. During its first
three years Kestrel operated from a single office in Leicester and financed its lending through a revolving
credit facility provided by its founding shareholders and a bilateral bank facility.

From 2008 Kestrel extended its dealer network to the south of England and Wales and, in 2011, opened a regional
office in Glasgow to serve dealers in Scotland. In 2013 Kestrel introduced its PCP product, which had until then
been offered principally by captive lenders, and established a residual value function to set and monitor
guaranteed future values (see section 9). In 2016 Kestrel began to finance dealers in Northern Ireland through
a team based in Belfast. In 2018 the Parent was incorporated as the new holding company of the group, and
Kestrel's head office moved to its present location in Leicester, where its underwriting, servicing,
collections and finance functions are located. Kestrel has no operations outside the United Kingdom.

In 2019 Kestrel completed the migration of its loan origination and administration to the systems described
below under "Systems". In 2021 it launched an online application journey through which a consumer who has
selected a vehicle at a dealer may complete the application, receive a decision and sign the Finance Agreement
electronically. Since 2022 Kestrel has offered dedicated finance for battery electric and plug-in hybrid
vehicles, for which it applies separate residual value curves.

## Business volumes

Kestrel's new business is generated exclusively through motor dealers that have entered into a dealer
agreement with Kestrel and that are themselves authorised by the FCA for credit broking or are appointed
representatives of a firm so authorised (each an "Introducing Dealer"). Kestrel does not lend directly to
consumers who approach it without an Introducing Dealer, other than through refinancing of the balloon payment
of an existing PCP Agreement, and agreements arising from such refinancing are not included in the Portfolio.

At 30 September 2026 Kestrel had dealer agreements with approximately 2,300 Introducing Dealers, of which
approximately 40% by number were franchised dealers and the remainder independent dealers. No single dealer
group introduced more than 4% of Kestrel's new business by amount advanced in the year ended 31 December 2025.
The following table shows Kestrel's new business by year. The figures are unaudited management information,
include agreements that are not eligible for the Transaction and are given for information only.

| Year ended 31 December | Agreements written | Amount advanced (£ million) | Of which PCP (£ million) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| 2021 | 41,200 | 552 | 318 |
| 2022 | 45,900 | 641 | 389 |
| 2023 | 48,300 | 702 | 437 |
| 2024 | 50,700 | 756 | 476 |
| 2025 | 53,100 | 811 | 519 |

At 30 September 2026 Kestrel administered a portfolio of approximately 141,000 live Finance Agreements with an
aggregate outstanding principal balance of approximately £1.62 billion, all of which were originated by Kestrel.
The Kestrel Group's consolidated audited financial statements for the year ended 31 December 2025 reported
profit before tax of £38.4 million and total equity of £214.7 million. The financial statements of the Parent
and of Kestrel are filed at Companies House and are available on request from the registered office of the
Parent.

Kestrel finances its lending through equity, subordinated loans from the Parent, a senior revolving warehouse
facility provided by a UK bank and secured on a portfolio of Finance Agreements held by a special purpose
company within the Kestrel Group, and bilateral term facilities. Certain of the Loans in the Portfolio were,
until the Closing Date, held by that special purpose company and will be released from the security of the
warehouse facility and transferred back to Kestrel immediately before their sale to the Issuer on the Closing
Date. The Transaction is part of Kestrel's strategy of diversifying its funding by accessing the term
securitisation market.

## Management

The board of directors of Kestrel consists of five executive directors and three non-executive directors, two
of whom are independent. The board is supported by an audit committee, a risk committee, a remuneration
committee and a customer and conduct committee, each chaired by a non-executive director. The principal
executive officers of Kestrel are as follows.

| Name | Position | Joined Kestrel |
|---|---|---:|
| Catherine Ellwood | Chief Executive Officer | 2004 |
| Daniel Prothero | Chief Financial Officer | 2012 |
| Rachel Okonjo | Chief Risk Officer | 2015 |
| Martin Haldane | Chief Operating Officer | 2009 |
| Priya Ramsden | Director of Customer Operations and Collections | 2017 |
| Owen Treharne | Chief Commercial Officer | 2010 |

Catherine Ellwood is a founder of Kestrel and has worked in UK motor finance for more than twenty-five years;
before founding Kestrel she held senior credit and sales positions in the motor finance division of a UK
bank. Daniel Prothero is a chartered accountant and was previously finance director of a UK consumer lender.
Rachel Okonjo is responsible for credit risk, residual value risk, operational risk and compliance, and
chairs the Credit Committee described in section 9. Martin Haldane is responsible for systems, change and
information security. Priya Ramsden is responsible for customer service, arrears management, recoveries and
vehicle remarketing. Owen Treharne is responsible for dealer relationships and the dealer support teams. The
business address of each of these persons is the registered office of Kestrel.

Kestrel employed approximately 640 full-time equivalent staff at 30 September 2026, of whom approximately 260
work in customer operations and collections, 70 in underwriting and credit risk, 55 in dealer support and the
remainder in finance, risk, compliance, systems and central functions. Each person in a customer-facing role,
and each person who exercises judgement in the collection of arrears, is subject to the certification regime
of the Senior Managers and Certification Regime and to annual competence assessment.

## Systems

Kestrel uses a single loan management platform, licensed from an established vendor of consumer lending
software and hosted in two UK data centres operated by a third party, for the administration of every Finance
Agreement from proposal to settlement. The platform holds the customer and vehicle records, the contractual
payment schedule, the balloon payment and guaranteed future value of each PCP Agreement, the payment history,
arrears status, forbearance arrangements and recovery history, and generates the statutory notices and
statements required by the CCA. Applications are received from Introducing Dealers through a web-based dealer
portal and are routed to an automated decision engine which retrieves credit reference agency data, applies
Kestrel's scorecards and policy rules and returns a decision or refers the application to an underwriter (see
section 9).

Collections are managed through a dialler and workflow system integrated with the loan management platform,
which assigns accounts in arrears to collection strategies and records each contact with the customer.
Payments by Direct Debit are submitted through the Bacs scheme by Kestrel as a service user; card payments are
processed through a payment service provider. Data on the loan management platform are replicated in real time
to the secondary data centre, and a full backup is taken daily and held off-site. Kestrel tests its business
continuity and disaster recovery arrangements at least annually; the most recent test, completed in June 2026,
restored the loan management platform and the payment files at the secondary site within the recovery time
objective set by the board. Kestrel's systems will produce the loan-level data required for the reports
described in section 13 and section 18 in the templates required by the Securitisation Regulations 2024 (SI
2024/102) and the FCA's securitisation rules.

The internal audit function of the Kestrel Group, which reports to the chair of the audit committee, reviews
the underwriting, servicing and collections processes on a risk-based cycle. The external auditors of Kestrel
have not been asked to report on the systems or controls described in this section. An independent firm of
accountants has, at the request of the Arranger, performed agreed-upon procedures on a sample of the Loans in
the Portfolio, comparing the data in the loan management platform with the underlying Finance Agreements and
with the data tape from which section 10 was prepared; no material exceptions were noted that have not been
corrected or the related Loans removed from the Portfolio.

## Previous securitisations

The Transaction is the first public securitisation of Kestrel and the first transaction in which Finance
Agreements originated by Kestrel have been securitised in a transaction rated by any rating agency. Before the
Closing Date Kestrel's securitisation experience consisted of the private warehouse facility described above
under "Business volumes", for the administration of which Kestrel has acted as servicer since 2017 and under
which it has delivered monthly loan-level and portfolio reports to the warehouse lender. Kestrel has
therefore not previously acted as servicer of a rated securitisation, and investors have no history of
investor reports for Kestrel-originated receivables. Historical static and dynamic performance data on
Kestrel's originations, covering a period of at least five years, have been made available to prospective
investors before pricing as described in section 18.

Kestrel has confirmed to the Issuer that it has expertise in originating and servicing exposures of a similar
nature to the Loans, through its management body and senior staff with professional experience in the
origination and servicing of motor finance receivables of more than five years, and that it has
well-documented and adequate policies, procedures and risk management controls relating to the servicing of
such exposures. Kestrel will act as the originator for the purposes of the Securitisation Regulations 2024 (SI
2024/102) and will retain a material net economic interest in the Transaction by way of the vertical slice
described in section 1 and section 18.

## Regulatory matters and the FCA review of motor finance commission

Kestrel is subject to the FCA's Principles for Businesses, including the Consumer Duty set out in Principle 12
and PRIN 2A, to the Consumer Credit sourcebook ("CONC") and to the other parts of the FCA Handbook that apply to
consumer credit firms. Kestrel has a compliance function independent of its business lines and reports to the
FCA through the regular regulatory returns required of consumer credit firms. Kestrel has not been the subject
of any FCA enforcement action or of any requirement imposed under section 55L of FSMA, and is not aware of any
investigation by the FCA into its business other than the matters described in this paragraph and the
following paragraphs.

Like other UK motor finance providers, Kestrel is affected by the FCA's review of historical commission
arrangements between lenders and motor dealers, by the related litigation in the English courts, including the
judgment of the Supreme Court in 2025 on the duties owed by motor dealers to customers and the disclosure of
commission, and by the industry-wide consumer redress scheme which the FCA has consulted on and is
implementing in respect of motor finance agreements entered into during the period covered by the scheme.
Before the FCA's prohibition of discretionary commission arrangements took effect in January 2021, a
proportion of Kestrel's agreements were introduced by dealers who were remunerated under arrangements which
allowed the dealer to adjust the interest rate within a range set by Kestrel. Kestrel has also reviewed the
disclosure of commission made to customers under the fixed commission arrangements which it has used since then.

Kestrel has made provisions in its financial statements for the estimated cost of redress and of the
administration of complaints relating to commission, has paused final responses to such complaints where
permitted by the FCA and is preparing to implement the redress scheme in the form in which it is finally
adopted. The amount of any redress, and the number of agreements affected, cannot yet be determined with
certainty and may differ materially from the amount provided. Some of the Loans in the Portfolio were entered
into during the period covered by the redress scheme. Under the Sale Agreement, Kestrel has agreed that it
remains liable for, and will indemnify the Issuer against, any redress, compensation, interest or costs
payable to a Borrower in connection with commission paid or payable to an Introducing Dealer in respect of a
Loan, and that any reduction of the amount owed by a Borrower which is made by way of redress will be treated
as a deemed collection which Kestrel must pay to the Issuer (see section 12). Investors should nevertheless
consider the risk factor in section 2 relating to the FCA review of motor finance commission, because the
Issuer's protection depends on the continued solvency of Kestrel and because a Borrower may in some
circumstances seek to set off a claim against amounts owed under a Loan.

Kestrel is not, and has not been during the twelve months before the date of this Prospectus, involved in any
governmental, legal or arbitration proceedings, other than the matters described above and complaints and
claims arising in the ordinary course of its business, which may have, or have had in the recent past,
significant effects on its financial position or its ability to perform its obligations under the
Transaction Documents. Complaints by Borrowers which Kestrel does not resolve may be referred to the Financial
Ombudsman Service; the number of complaints referred to, and upheld by, the Financial Ombudsman Service in
relation to Kestrel is published by the Financial Ombudsman Service in accordance with its usual practice.

## Kestrel as Servicer

Kestrel will service the Loans on behalf of the Issuer under the servicing agreement dated the Closing Date
between Pellam Corporate Services Ltd (the "Back-up Servicer Facilitator"), the Issuer, Kestrel and the Trustee
(the "Servicing Agreement"), in accordance with the same policies and procedures that it applies to Finance
Agreements which it owns for its own account. Kestrel will continue to service the Loans from its head office in
Leicester using the systems and staff described above, and Borrowers will continue to deal with Kestrel as
before; they will not be notified of the sale of the Loans unless a Perfection Event occurs (see section 12).
The servicing, collection and arrears procedures, the reports which the Servicer will deliver and the
circumstances in which its appointment may be terminated are described in section 13. The fee payable to the
Servicer is set out in section 15.

# 9. Origination and underwriting

The information in this section has been provided by Kestrel Motor Finance Ltd ("Kestrel"). It summarises the
origination and underwriting policies and procedures (the "Credit and Collection Policies") which applied to the
Loans in the Portfolio when they were originated and which Kestrel applies to new Finance Agreements at the date
of this Prospectus. The Credit and Collection Policies are reviewed at least annually by the Credit Committee
and may be amended from time to time; the restrictions on changes to the Credit and Collection Policies after
the Closing Date are described in section 13. Each Loan was originated in accordance with the Credit and
Collection Policies in force at the date of its origination, as warranted by Kestrel under the Sale Agreement
(see section 12). The criteria which a Loan had to satisfy to be included in the Portfolio are the Eligibility
Criteria set out in section 11; they are narrower than the lending criteria described in this section, and
not every Finance Agreement which Kestrel originates is eligible for the Transaction.

## Governance of credit risk

Kestrel's credit risk appetite is set by its board on the recommendation of the risk committee. Within that
appetite, the credit committee of Kestrel (the "Credit Committee"), chaired by the Chief Risk Officer and
including the Chief Financial Officer, the Chief Operating Officer and the heads of underwriting, credit
analytics and residual values, approves the scorecards, the policy rules, the cut-off scores, the affordability
model, the maximum advance and loan-to-value limits by product and vehicle age, the residual value curves and
the delegated lending authorities described below. The Credit Committee meets monthly and reviews management
information on application volumes, approval rates, early arrears, defaults, losses on repossessed and returned
vehicles and the performance of each dealer segment. A change to a scorecard, a cut-off score or the
affordability model is validated by the credit analytics team, which is independent of the sales function,
and is monitored after implementation by comparison of actual and expected performance.

Kestrel's commercial teams have no lending authority. Sales and dealer support staff are not remunerated by
reference to the volume or value of business approved, and no element of the remuneration of any underwriter
depends on the number of applications approved.

## Dealer onboarding and monitoring

Kestrel accepts applications only from Introducing Dealers. Before Kestrel enters into a dealer agreement, its
dealer onboarding team carries out due diligence on the prospective dealer, which includes:

(a) confirmation that the dealer is authorised by the FCA for credit broking, or is an appointed representative
of a principal firm so authorised, and that the scope of its permission or appointment covers the introduction
of regulated credit agreements;

(b) verification of the identity of the dealer's legal entity, its directors and its beneficial owners, and
screening of each of them against sanctions, politically exposed persons and adverse media lists, in accordance
with the Money Laundering, Terrorist Financing and Transfer of Funds (Information on the Payer) Regulations 2017;

(c) a review of the dealer's financial position, using filed accounts and commercial credit reference agency
data, and of any county court judgments, insolvency events or disqualifications affecting the dealer or its
directors;

(d) a site visit or a remote inspection of the dealer's premises, stock and sales processes, and a review of
its website and advertising for compliance with the financial promotion rules in CONC 3;

(e) a review of the dealer's complaints handling procedures and of any complaints about the dealer referred to
the Financial Ombudsman Service; and

(f) confirmation that the dealer's sales staff who will introduce applications have completed Kestrel's
training on its products, the disclosure of commission, the treatment of vulnerable customers and the
prevention of fraud.

Each dealer agreement requires the Introducing Dealer to comply with CONC and with the Consumer Duty, to
disclose to the customer the existence and nature of any commission payable by Kestrel in accordance with the
FCA's rules, to provide the customer with the pre-contract information and explanations required by the CCA and
CONC 4, to verify the identity of the customer and to deliver to the customer a vehicle which conforms to its
description and to the Consumer Rights Act 2015. Commission is paid to Introducing Dealers on a fixed basis by
reference to the amount of credit and the product; Kestrel has not permitted any dealer to vary the interest
rate charged to a customer since the FCA's prohibition of discretionary commission arrangements took effect.
The Introducing Dealer gives warranties as to the vehicle and the information supplied, and indemnifies Kestrel
against losses arising from a breach of the dealer agreement; Kestrel may claw back commission on agreements
that are cancelled, withdrawn or terminated early.

Kestrel monitors each Introducing Dealer through monthly management information on the volume and quality of
its applications, approval and decline rates, cancellations and withdrawals, early payment default (a missed
instalment in the first months of an agreement), complaints, fraud referrals and losses on vehicles. A dealer
whose indicators deviate from those of its peer group is placed on a watch list and reviewed by the dealer risk
team, which may require additional documents on every application from that dealer, suspend its ability to
introduce business or terminate the dealer agreement. Each Introducing Dealer is subject to an annual review of
its FCA status and, for dealers above a volume threshold set by the Credit Committee, a periodic audit of a
sample of files. Kestrel checks the FCA status of all Introducing Dealers against the Financial Services Register
each month.

## The application process

A customer who wishes to finance a vehicle through an Introducing Dealer completes an application, either with
the dealer at its premises or remotely through Kestrel's online application journey. The application captures
the customer's identity, date of birth, address history for the previous three years, marital and residential
status, employment status, employer and length of employment, gross and net income, the number of dependants,
the customer's declared regular expenditure and existing credit commitments, the vehicle (including its
registration or chassis number, mileage, age and the dealer's cash price), the deposit (in cash, by
part-exchange or from a manufacturer or dealer deposit contribution), the requested term and, for a PCP
Agreement, the annual mileage which the customer expects to drive. The customer's consent is obtained before
any search is made with a credit reference agency, and a quotation search is used until the customer proceeds
with a full application.

The dealer submits the application through the dealer portal. The decision engine verifies the vehicle against
an industry vehicle data service, which confirms its identity, its first registration date, whether it has been
recorded as stolen, written off or subject to outstanding finance, and its current trade valuation. The engine
then retrieves the customer's credit file from one or more credit reference agencies and applies the policy
rules, the scorecard, the affordability assessment and the fraud checks described below. It returns one of
three outcomes to the dealer: an acceptance, which may be conditional on proof of income, proof of address or
another document; a referral to an underwriter; or a decline. Where an application is declined, the customer is
told that the decision has been made and, where the decision was based on information from a credit reference
agency, is given the name and contact details of that agency as required by section 157 of the CCA.

When an application has been accepted, the dealer presents the pre-contract credit information in the Standard
European Consumer Credit Information form prescribed under the Consumer Credit (Disclosure of Information)
Regulations 2010 and the adequate explanations required by CONC 4.2, and the customer signs the Finance
Agreement, either on paper or electronically through Kestrel's e-signature process. Kestrel pays the dealer the
amount financed only after it has received the signed Finance Agreement and the supporting documents, confirmed
that any conditions of acceptance have been met and recorded the vehicle as subject to its finance agreement
with the industry vehicle finance register. The customer may withdraw from the Finance Agreement within
fourteen days under section 66A of the CCA, in which case the customer must repay the credit with accrued
interest; no Loan was within that period at the Cut-off Date, because each Loan had the seasoning required by
the Eligibility Criteria in section 11.

## Credit scoring

Kestrel uses application scorecards developed by its credit analytics team on its own historical performance
data, which combine credit reference agency data (including payment history, current indebtedness, recent
credit searches, county court judgments, insolvency records and electoral roll data) with application data and
the characteristics of the vehicle and the proposed agreement. Separate scorecards are used for new and used
vehicles and for applicants with limited credit histories. The scorecard produces a score which is mapped to a
risk grade. Applications are subject to minimum scores and to policy rules set by the Credit Committee, which
decline, among others, applicants under 18 years of age, applicants not resident in the United Kingdom,
applicants who are subject to a current bankruptcy order, sequestration, individual voluntary arrangement,
debt relief order or trust deed, and applicants with unsatisfied county court judgments above a threshold amount
or recent defaults. The credit score recorded in the data tape and used in section 10 is the score of the
credit reference agency at origination and not Kestrel's internal score.

Each scorecard is monitored monthly for its stability and its power to rank applicants by risk. A scorecard
whose performance deteriorates beyond the tolerance set by the Credit Committee is recalibrated or redeveloped.
The interest rate charged on a Finance Agreement depends on the product, the vehicle and the risk grade, within
a rate card approved by the Credit Committee; the Introducing Dealer has no discretion over the rate.

## Creditworthiness and affordability

Kestrel carries out a creditworthiness assessment of every applicant in accordance with CONC 5.2A before it
enters into a Finance Agreement or significantly increases the amount of credit. The assessment considers both
the risk to Kestrel that the customer will not make repayments (credit risk) and the risk to the customer of
not being able to make repayments without undue difficulty, that is, without failing to meet other reasonable
commitments, without having to borrow further to meet the repayments and without a significant adverse impact
on the customer's financial situation (affordability risk). The assessment is proportionate to the amount and
the term of the credit and to the customer's circumstances, and is based on sufficient information obtained
from the customer and from credit reference agencies.

Kestrel verifies income in every case. For most applicants, income is verified through a credit reference agency
service which compares declared income with the turnover recorded on the applicant's current account; where
that check does not confirm income within a tolerance, the applicant is asked for payslips, bank statements or,
for a self-employed applicant, tax calculations or accounts. Committed expenditure, including existing credit
repayments and housing costs, is taken from the credit file and the application; essential living costs are
estimated using statistical data published by the Office for National Statistics, adjusted for household size
and region, and the higher of the declared and the estimated figure is used. The application is declined
unless the customer's verified net income after committed expenditure, essential living costs and a buffer for
unexpected costs is sufficient to meet the proposed instalment. The assessment uses the scheduled instalments
and, for a PCP Agreement, takes into account that the customer will have to pay, refinance or settle the
balloon payment through the return of the vehicle at the end of the term.

Kestrel does not take into account the value of the vehicle as a substitute for the customer's ability to repay.
The customer is asked about any foreseeable changes in income or expenditure during the term of the agreement,
such as retirement or the end of a fixed-term contract, and the assessment is adjusted accordingly.

## Fraud prevention

Each application is checked against the fraud prevention databases to which Kestrel subscribes, the internal
records of previous applications to Kestrel and the credit reference agency identity and address verification
services. The decision engine identifies inconsistencies in the application data, the reuse of contact details
across unrelated applications, mismatches between the applicant's address and the dealer's location and other
indicators of application fraud, impersonation and first-party fraud. Applications which trigger a fraud rule
are referred to Kestrel's fraud team, which may contact the applicant, request original documents or conduct a
video identity check. Confirmed frauds are recorded on the fraud prevention databases, and Introducing Dealers
associated with repeated fraud are reviewed under the dealer monitoring process. Kestrel also verifies, before
paying out, that the vehicle is not recorded as stolen or as subject to existing finance and that the dealer is
entitled to sell it, and checks that the amount financed is consistent with the trade valuation of the vehicle.

## Loan-to-value and the amount of credit

The amount of credit under each Finance Agreement is the cash price of the vehicle, plus any optional products
financed with Kestrel's approval, less the deposit. Kestrel sets maximum loan-to-value ratios by product, by
vehicle age and by risk grade, by reference to the trade valuation of the vehicle at origination supplied by the
vehicle data service; for the purposes of section 10, the original loan-to-value of a Loan is the amount of
credit divided by that valuation. Kestrel requires a minimum deposit, set by the Credit Committee, on all PCP
Agreements and on HP Agreements for higher risk grades. Kestrel finances only cars and light commercial vehicles
under a maximum age at the end of the agreement and a maximum mileage at origination. Agreements for light
commercial vehicles and agreements with an original loan-to-value above 100% are excluded from the Portfolio by
the Eligibility Criteria in section 11. The original term of an HP Agreement or a PCP Agreement
is between 12 and 60 months.

## Personal contract purchase and the setting of residual values

Under a PCP Agreement the customer pays a deposit, a series of monthly instalments and a final optional payment
(the "Balloon Payment"). The Balloon Payment is equal to the guaranteed minimum future value of the vehicle at the
end of the agreement (the "GMFV"), set by Kestrel at origination for the term and the annual mileage agreed with
the customer. At the end of the term the customer may pay the Balloon Payment and acquire title to the vehicle,
or return the vehicle to Kestrel in full satisfaction of the Balloon Payment, provided that the vehicle is
within the agreed mileage and is in a condition consistent with fair wear and tear; if it is not, the customer
pays an excess mileage charge set out in the agreement and the cost of repairing damage. A customer may also
part-exchange the vehicle at a dealer, in which case the Balloon Payment is settled from the sale proceeds. In
addition, a customer under any Finance Agreement may terminate it voluntarily under sections 99 and 100 of the
CCA, as described in section 13.

Kestrel's residual value function sets the GMFV for each make, model, derivative, fuel type, term and mileage
band. The GMFV is derived from the forecast future trade values of an independent vehicle valuation provider,
adjusted by Kestrel for its own historical experience of returned vehicles and for the residual value appetite
set by the Credit Committee. Kestrel applies a prudence margin below the forecast future trade value for every
vehicle; the margin is larger for vehicles with less established resale markets, including battery electric
vehicles, for which separate residual value curves and lower maximum GMFVs have applied since 2022. The residual
value function reviews the forecasts monthly and, where the forecast values for a segment fall, may reduce
the GMFVs offered on new agreements in that segment. The GMFV of an existing PCP Agreement is not changed after
origination.

Kestrel measures its residual value exposure monthly by comparing the Balloon Payment of each live PCP Agreement
with the current forecast trade value of the vehicle at maturity, and reports the results to the Credit
Committee and the risk committee. The Issuer bears the risk that the value of a returned vehicle is lower than the
Balloon Payment it replaces, as described in section 2 and section 13.

## Approval authorities and referrals

Applications which pass the policy rules, the scorecard, the affordability assessment and the fraud checks, and
which are within the automatic approval limits for the risk grade, are approved by the decision engine. All
other applications which are not declined automatically are referred to an underwriter. Underwriters exercise
lending authority delegated by the Credit Committee to named individuals according to their grade, as follows.
The amounts are Kestrel's internal limits on the amount of credit and are not terms of the Notes.

| Authority | Maximum amount of credit | May approve exceptions to policy |
|---|---:|---|
| Underwriter | £30,000 | No |
| Senior underwriter | £50,000 | Minor exceptions only |
| Underwriting manager | £75,000 | Yes, within the limits of the policy |
| Head of underwriting or Chief Risk Officer | Above £75,000 | Yes |

An underwriter who refers an application considers the information on the credit file, the affordability
assessment and any documents supplied, and may approve it, approve it on amended terms (for example with a
higher deposit, a shorter term or a different vehicle), request further information or decline it. The reasons
for each decision are recorded on the loan management platform. An underwriter may not approve an application
introduced by a dealer with which the underwriter has a personal connection, and a sample of decisions by each
underwriter is reviewed each month by the quality assurance team.

## Exceptions to policy

An exception is an approval of an application which does not meet one or more of the policy rules, other than
the rules which give effect to legal or regulatory requirements, which may not be waived. Exceptions may be
approved only by an underwriter with authority to approve them and only where the underwriter records a reason
which mitigates the risk, such as a strong payment history with Kestrel, verified income substantially in excess
of the minimum required, or a larger deposit. Exceptions are reported to the Credit Committee each month, and
the number of exceptions is limited by the Credit Committee to a small proportion of approvals. The performance
of agreements approved as exceptions is monitored separately. No exception may be made to the affordability
assessment.

## Quality assurance and the underwriting audit

Kestrel's quality assurance team, which reports to the Chief Risk Officer, reviews each month a sample of
applications approved automatically and of applications decided by underwriters, and checks that the documents
required by the Credit and Collection Policies were obtained, that the affordability assessment was completed
correctly and that the Finance Agreement was executed in the prescribed form. Findings are reported to the
Credit Committee, and remedial training is given where necessary. The Kestrel Group's internal audit function
reviews the underwriting process at least every two years.

## Documentation and retention of records

Each Finance Agreement is executed in the form required by the Consumer Credit (Agreements) Regulations 2010 and
contains the prescribed terms required for its enforceability under the CCA. Kestrel maintains a standard
form of HP Agreement and of PCP Agreement which is reviewed by its legal and compliance functions whenever the
law or the FCA's rules change; the form of each Finance Agreement is confirmed by Kestrel's warranty in the Sale
Agreement (see section 12). Agreements signed electronically are executed through an e-signature platform which
records the identity checks performed, the time of signature and a tamper-evident copy of the signed document;
the customer is provided with a copy of the executed agreement in a durable medium.

Kestrel holds the original or electronic signed Finance Agreement, the application, the credit file at
origination, the affordability assessment, the identity and income verification documents, the dealer's
invoice and the vehicle data check (together, the "Loan Files") in electronic form in its document management
system, with paper originals held in a secure archive operated by a third party. Under the Servicing Agreement,
Kestrel holds the Loan Files relating to the Loans as agent of the Issuer and must make them available to the
Issuer, the Trustee and any successor servicer on request. Kestrel retains Loan Files for at least six years
after the end of the relevant Finance Agreement, in accordance with its record-keeping obligations under the
FCA Handbook, the limitation periods applicable under the Limitation Act 1980 and its policies under the UK
General Data Protection Regulation and the Data Protection Act 2018, after which they are securely destroyed
unless they relate to a complaint, a claim or a regulatory review which is still open.

# 11. Eligibility criteria and definitions
Each loan met the following criteria at the cut-off date:

1. The borrower is an individual resident in the United Kingdom.
2. The loan is a hire purchase or personal contract purchase agreement for a car.
3. No instalment was in arrears (days past due equal to 0).
4. At least 3 instalments had been paid (seasoning of 3 months or more).
5. At least 6 instalments remained to be paid.
6. The original loan-to-value was 100% or less.

**Definitions used in this prospectus**

- **Pool balance**: Sum of the outstanding principal of all loans that are not defaulted, prepaid or repaid.
- **Defaulted loan**: A loan is defaulted when it is 4 instalments in arrears. A defaulted loan leaves the pool balance in the period
  in which it defaults; its outstanding principal at that date is its *defaulted balance*.
- **Recoveries**: amounts received after a default, mainly from the sale of the vehicle. For this transaction
  the servicer expects to recover 55% of the defaulted balance,
  3 periods after the default.
- **Cumulative default ratio**: the sum of the defaulted balances of all loans that have defaulted since the
  cut-off date, divided by the pool balance at the cut-off date (£74,075,862.24).
- **90+ day arrears ratio**: the total balance of loans that are not defaulted and have 3 instalments in
  arrears, divided by the pool balance, both measured at the end of the period.
- **Available funds**: for each period, the sum of interest collections, principal received from instalments
  (including balloon payments), prepayments and recoveries received during the period.
