# 12. Loan warranties and repurchase

## The Sale Agreement

On the Closing Date, Kestrel Motor Finance Ltd ("Kestrel", in this capacity the "Seller") will sell the Loans in
the Portfolio to Thames Auto Receivables 2026-1 plc (the "Issuer") under a receivables sale agreement dated the
Closing Date between the Seller, the Issuer and the Trustee (the "Sale Agreement"). The sale takes effect, in
relation to each Loan, from the Cut-off Date, so that all collections received by the Seller on or after the
Cut-off Date in respect of the Loans belong to the Issuer, whether received before or after the Closing Date.
The Sale Agreement is governed by English law, save that the declaration of trust by which the Seller holds the
Loans whose Borrowers reside in Scotland (the "Scottish Loans") on trust for the Issuer (the "Scottish
Declaration of Trust") is governed by Scots law, as is required for it to take effect.

The property sold comprises, for each Loan, the Seller's rights under the related HP Agreement or PCP Agreement
to receive all instalments, Balloon Payments, settlement amounts, arrears, fees and other sums payable by the
Borrower, together with the Seller's rights against the Introducing Dealer and any insurer in relation to the
Loan, the proceeds of any claim, and the Seller's rights in respect of the financed vehicle, including the
proceeds of its sale after repossession, surrender, voluntary termination or return at the end of a PCP
Agreement (together, the "Ancillary Rights"). Title to each vehicle remains registered in the records of the
Seller. Because the transfer of legal title to vehicles would require notification of each Borrower, the Seller
will declare a trust over its title to the vehicles and over the proceeds of their sale in favour of the Issuer
under the Sale Agreement (the "Vehicle Trust"), and will hold that title, and apply any proceeds, as trustee for
the Issuer.

The consideration for the sale consists of an initial purchase price paid in cash on the Closing Date from the
proceeds of the issue of the Notes (the "Initial Purchase Price"), equal to the aggregate outstanding principal
of the Loans at the Cut-off Date, and of deferred consideration payable to the Seller on each Payment Date to the
extent of the Available Funds remaining after the payment of the items ranking in priority to it in the Priority
of Payments described in section 15 (the "Deferred Consideration"). The Seller has no claim against the Issuer
for any Deferred Consideration which is not paid because the Available Funds are insufficient.

The Sale Agreement contains the representations and warranties described below, the Seller's obligation to
repurchase Loans which do not comply with them, the obligations of the Seller relating to commission redress
and set-off described in section 8, and the provisions on the perfection of the Issuer's title.

## Representations of the Seller as to itself

The Seller represents and warrants to the Issuer and the Trustee on the date of the Sale Agreement and on the
Closing Date that:

(a) it is duly incorporated and validly existing under the laws of England and Wales and has the power to
enter into and perform the Transaction Documents to which it is a party;

(b) its obligations under those Transaction Documents are legal, valid, binding and enforceable, subject to
the laws on insolvency and creditors' rights and to the general principles of law;

(c) it holds all authorisations required under FSMA and the CCA to originate, own and service the Loans, and
no such authorisation has been suspended, varied or cancelled;

(d) it is not unable to pay its debts within the meaning of section 123 of the Insolvency Act 1986 and will not
become so as a result of the sale of the Loans, which is made for full value and in good faith.

## Loan warranties

In addition, the Seller will represent and warrant to the Issuer and the Trustee in relation to each Loan, as at
the Cut-off Date or, where stated, the Closing Date (together, the "Loan Warranties"), that the Loan satisfied
each of the Eligibility Criteria set out in section 11 and that:

(a) the Loan was originated by the Seller in the ordinary course of its business, in accordance with the Credit
and Collection Policies described in section 9 as they applied at the date of origination, and through an
Introducing Dealer which was, at that date, authorised by the FCA, or an appointed representative of a firm so
authorised, for the introduction of regulated credit agreements;

(b) the Finance Agreement was entered into in the Seller's standard form of HP Agreement or PCP Agreement in
use at that date, contains all prescribed terms and complies in all material respects with the CCA and the
regulations made under it, so that it is enforceable against the Borrower without an order of the court under
section 127 of the CCA;

(c) the pre-contract information and the adequate explanations required by the CCA and CONC were given to the
Borrower, a creditworthiness assessment was carried out in accordance with CONC 5.2A, and the Borrower's right of
withdrawal under section 66A of the CCA has expired without being exercised;

(d) the Seller is the sole legal and beneficial owner of the Loan and of the Ancillary Rights and has full title
to the vehicle, free from any security interest, trust, option or other third party right, other than the
rights of the Borrower under the Finance Agreement and any security which is released on or before the Closing
Date;

(e) the Loan and the Ancillary Rights are capable of being sold to the Issuer, and of being held on trust for
it, without the consent of the Borrower or any other person and without breach of any restriction on transfer;

(f) the Loan bears interest at a fixed rate, is denominated and payable in sterling, and is repayable by monthly
instalments and, for a PCP Agreement, a Balloon Payment equal to the GMFV set at origination;

(g) the vehicle was a car which, at origination, was not recorded as stolen, written off or subject to
outstanding finance, was delivered to the Borrower and accepted by the Borrower, and is registered with the
industry vehicle finance register as subject to the Finance Agreement;

(h) no Borrower has notified the Seller of any rejection of the vehicle under the Consumer Rights Act 2015, of
any claim under section 75 or section 56 of the CCA or of any claim for rescission, and, so far as the Seller is
aware, no Borrower has a right of set-off or counterclaim against the Seller, other than a claim in respect of
commission which is subject to the redress scheme and indemnity described in section 8;

(i) the Seller has not received notice of the death or bankruptcy of any Borrower, or of any voluntary
termination under section 99 of the CCA, and the Loan is not subject to a forbearance arrangement, a payment
holiday or a variation of its contractual terms which remains in effect at the Cut-off Date;

(j) the Loan is not a Defaulted Loan, has not been the subject of a default notice under section 87 of the CCA
and has not been written off in whole or in part;

(k) the Borrower is not an employee, director or connected person of the Seller and is not, so far as the
Seller is aware, the subject of any insolvency proceedings;

(l) the Loan Files for the Loan are complete in all material respects and are held by the Seller in accordance
with section 9, and the information relating to the Loan in the data tape from which section 10 was prepared
is true and accurate in all material respects;

(m) the Loan has been serviced by the Seller since its origination in accordance with the Credit and Collection
Policies, the CCA, CONC and the Consumer Duty, and the Seller has complied in all material respects with its
obligations to send the statements, notices and information required by the CCA; and

(n) the Loan has not been selected for sale to the Issuer by a process intended to select assets of lower credit
quality than comparable Finance Agreements of the Seller which satisfy the Eligibility Criteria.

The Loan Warranties are given by reference to the facts at the Cut-off Date or the Closing Date and are not
repeated after the Closing Date. The Seller gives no warranty as to the future performance of any Loan, the
creditworthiness of any Borrower or the value of any vehicle, and the Issuer bears the credit risk and the
residual value risk of the Loans, subject only to the remedies described below. The Issuer and the Trustee rely on the Loan Warranties and have made no investigation of the Loans other
than the agreed-upon procedures described in section 8.

## Repurchase of non-compliant Loans

If the Seller, the Servicer, the Issuer, the Cash Manager or the Trustee becomes aware that a Loan Warranty was
untrue in relation to a Loan when it was given, it must notify the other parties promptly. Where the breach has a
material adverse effect on the value of the Loan or on the interests of the Issuer in it, and, if capable of
remedy, is not remedied within 30 days of the Seller becoming aware of it or receiving notice of it, the Seller
must repurchase the Loan (a "Non-Compliant Loan") together with its Ancillary Rights on or before the Payment Date
following the end of that period. A breach of the warranty that a Loan satisfied the Eligibility Criteria is
treated as having a material adverse effect, whatever the performance of the Loan.

The price payable by the Seller for a Non-Compliant Loan (the "Repurchase Price") is the outstanding principal
of the Loan on the date of repurchase, plus accrued and unpaid interest and any other amounts due from the
Borrower, plus the costs reasonably incurred by the Issuer in connection with the repurchase. Where the
Non-Compliant Loan has become a Defaulted Loan, the Repurchase Price is calculated on the outstanding principal
at the date on which it became a Defaulted Loan, less any Recoveries already received by the Issuer. The
Repurchase Price is paid into the Issuer Transaction Account described in section 14 and forms part of the
Available Funds for the relevant period, as principal received in respect of the outstanding principal and as
interest collections in respect of the remainder.

Where the Seller is unable to repurchase a Non-Compliant Loan, for example because the Finance Agreement has
been settled or the vehicle has been sold, the Seller must instead pay the Issuer an indemnity amount equal to
the Repurchase Price that would have been payable. The Seller's obligations to repurchase a Non-Compliant Loan
or pay that indemnity amount, and its indemnity in respect of commission redress and set-off described in
section 8, are the sole remedies of the Issuer and the Trustee for a breach of the Loan Warranties. These
obligations are unsecured obligations of the Seller and are not guaranteed by the Parent or any other person;
their value to the Issuer depends on the solvency of the Seller at the time of the claim, as explained in
section 2.

The Seller must also repurchase a Loan, at the Repurchase Price, if the Servicer agrees a variation of the
Finance Agreement which is not permitted by the Servicing Agreement (see section 13), and may repurchase all, but
not some only, of the Loans on any Payment Date on which the Issuer may redeem the Notes under the clean-up call
described in section 5, at a price which, together with the other Available Funds, is sufficient to redeem the
Notes in full and pay all amounts ranking in priority to them.

## No substitution and no further sales

The Portfolio is static. The Seller has no right or obligation to substitute another Finance Agreement for a
Non-Compliant Loan or for any other Loan, and the Issuer will not purchase any further loans after the Closing
Date. Apart from a repurchase described above, the Seller has no right to repurchase or call any Loan, and the
Issuer has no right to require the Seller to repurchase a Loan because it has become a Defaulted Loan or has
otherwise performed badly.

## Notification and perfection of the Issuer's title

The Seller will not, on the Closing Date, give notice of the sale to the Borrowers, the Introducing Dealers or
any insurer. Until notice is given, the sale of the Loans which are governed by English law or the law of
Northern Ireland takes effect as an equitable assignment, and the Issuer's interest in the Scottish Loans takes
effect under the Scottish Declaration of Trust. As a result, until notice is given or an assignation is
intimated:

(a) a Borrower may continue to pay the Seller and obtains a good discharge by doing so;

(b) a Borrower may set off against the Seller claims which arise before the Borrower receives notice, and the
Issuer's rights may be subject to equities which arise between the Borrower and the Seller;

(c) the Seller remains the legal owner of the Loans and the vehicles and must be joined in any proceedings
taken by the Issuer to enforce a Loan; and

(d) a later legal assignee of a Loan without notice of the Issuer's interest could obtain priority over it.

The Issuer is protected against these risks by the Seller's covenants in the Sale Agreement, the Vehicle Trust,
the declaration of trust over the Collection Account described in section 14 and the Seller's obligation to pay
any amount set off by a Borrower as a deemed collection. The Seller will grant to the Issuer and the Trustee
an irrevocable power of attorney, by way of security for its obligations, to perfect the transfer of the Loans
and the vehicles in its name after a Perfection Event.

Each of the following events is a "Perfection Event":

(a) a Servicer Termination Event occurs, as described in section 13, and the appointment of Kestrel as Servicer
is terminated;

(b) an Insolvency Event occurs in relation to the Seller;

(c) the Seller fails to pay any amount due under the Sale Agreement, including a Repurchase Price, within five
Business Days of its due date;

(d) the Trustee delivers an enforcement notice to the Issuer under the Conditions;

(e) the Seller is required to perfect the Issuer's title by law, by an order of a court or by a requirement of a
regulatory authority; or

(f) the Trustee, acting reasonably, considers that the security created by the Issuer, or the Issuer's title to
a material part of the Loans, is in jeopardy.

Within 20 Business Days after a Perfection Event, the Seller (or, if it fails to do so, the Issuer or the
Trustee under the power of attorney) must give notice of the assignment to each Borrower and instruct each
Borrower to pay directly into an account of the Issuer, execute legal assignments and assignations of the
Loans, intimate the assignation of the Scottish Loans to the Borrowers and transfer to the Issuer, or as it
directs, legal title to the vehicles and the Loan Files. The costs of perfection are borne by the Seller. After
notice, payments by a Borrower to the Seller do not discharge the Borrower, and new rights of set-off against
the Seller cease to arise. Notice to Borrowers will be given in a form which complies with the CCA, CONC and
the Consumer Duty, and the Borrowers' rights under their Finance Agreements, including the right to voluntary
termination and the protections described in section 13, will not be affected by the transfer.

\newpage

# 13. Servicing, collections and arrears

## Appointment of the Servicer

Under the Servicing Agreement, the Issuer and, after the delivery of an enforcement notice, the Trustee appoint
Kestrel Motor Finance Ltd ("Kestrel", in this capacity the "Servicer") to administer the Loans and the Ancillary
Rights, to collect all amounts payable in respect of them, to manage arrears, defaults and recoveries and to
report to the Issuer, the Cash Manager and the Trustee. The Servicer acts as agent of the Issuer and holds the
Loan Files, and any collections which it receives, for the Issuer. In performing its services the Servicer must
apply the Credit and Collection Policies described in section 9 and this section, with the degree of skill and
care which it applies to Finance Agreements which it services for its own account and which a reasonable and
prudent lender of motor finance in the United Kingdom would apply, and must comply with the CCA, FSMA, CONC,
the Consumer Duty and data protection law. The Servicer may subcontract specific services, such as the
collection of vehicles, the auction of vehicles, tracing and the printing and dispatch of statements, to third
parties, but remains liable for their performance.

The Servicer may amend the Credit and Collection Policies only where the amendment is required by law or by the
FCA, or where it would be made by a reasonable and prudent lender and it applies the amendment equally to
Finance Agreements which it services for its own account; it must give prior notice to the Issuer, the Trustee
and the Rating Agencies of any amendment which would be likely to have a material adverse effect on the Loans.
The Servicer may not vary the terms of a Loan except as permitted by the Servicing Agreement. Permitted
variations are a change of payment date or payment method, a forbearance arrangement agreed under the
procedures described below, a settlement or a voluntary termination exercised under the CCA, and any variation
required by law, by the FCA, by the Financial Ombudsman Service or by the redress scheme described in section 8.
No variation may reduce the interest rate of a Loan (other than as required by law or by such redress) or extend
its final scheduled payment beyond the date falling two years before the Final Maturity Date. A variation which
is not permitted obliges the Seller to repurchase the Loan (see section 12).

**Servicer fee.** The servicer fee is 1.00% per year of the pool balance, paid to the Servicer
monthly as a senior fee in the Priority of Payments (section 15). The Servicer bears from that fee all its costs of servicing, other than the costs of
repossession, transport, storage and sale of vehicles and legal costs of enforcement, which are deducted from
the proceeds of the relevant Loan before those proceeds are paid to the Issuer.

## Payment methods and Direct Debits

Each Borrower is required by the Finance Agreement to pay instalments monthly, and nearly all Borrowers pay by
direct debit under the Bacs Direct Debit scheme ("Direct Debit") on a collection date chosen by the Borrower at
origination. Borrowers may also pay by debit card, by standing order or by bank transfer, and may make
additional payments or settle the agreement early by any of these methods. The Servicer submits the Direct
Debit files on the working day before each collection date, and the funds are credited to the Collection Account
on the collection date. Under the Direct Debit Guarantee, a Borrower may require its bank to refund a payment
which was taken in error; the bank then reclaims the amount from the Servicer through an indemnity claim. Amounts
so reclaimed after they have been transferred to the Issuer are netted against later collections, as described
in section 14. Where a Direct Debit is returned unpaid, the Servicer re-presents it once, on a date agreed with
the Borrower where possible, and the account enters the arrears process described below.

A Borrower may settle a Finance Agreement early at any time under section 94 of the CCA by paying the settlement
amount calculated in accordance with the Consumer Credit (Early Settlement) Regulations 2004, which includes a
statutory rebate of interest. A Borrower may also make partial early settlements, which the Servicer applies
to reduce the term or the instalment as the Borrower chooses. Settlement amounts are principal received in
respect of the outstanding principal of the Loan and interest collections in respect of the remainder, and
are part of the Available Funds defined in section 11.

## Collection accounts

All payments by Borrowers under the Loans are paid into an account of the Servicer held with Larkmead Bank plc
(the "Collection Account Bank"), into which payments under other Finance Agreements serviced by Kestrel are also
paid (the "Collection Account"). Kestrel has declared a trust over the Collection Account in favour of the
Issuer, the beneficiaries under the other financing arrangements of Kestrel and Kestrel itself, according to
their respective interests, and the Servicer identifies the collections on the Loans each day. Collections on
the Loans are transferred to the Issuer Transaction Account on the Business Day after the day on which they are
identified. The declaration of trust, the daily sweep and the accounts of the Issuer are described in section
14.

## Arrears management

An account is in arrears when a contractual instalment has not been paid in full on its due date. The Servicer's
arrears management is organised in stages which reflect the time since the first missed instalment, the
amount in arrears and the circumstances of the Borrower. Throughout, the Servicer's objective, consistent with
the Consumer Duty and CONC 7, is to understand the reason for the arrears, to agree an affordable and
sustainable way for the Borrower to repay, and to avoid foreseeable harm to the Borrower, including the harm of
unaffordable arrangements or of an accumulation of charges. The Servicer charges a single fee for a returned
Direct Debit and does not charge fees for collection letters or calls; any fee charged in arrears reflects the
Servicer's reasonable costs as required by CONC 7.7.

*Early arrears.* When an instalment is missed, the Servicer sends a text message, an email or both on the
following day, re-presents the Direct Debit and writes to the Borrower. The early arrears team contacts the
Borrower by telephone and through Kestrel's digital channels, including an online portal in which the Borrower
may make a payment, propose an arrangement or complete an income and expenditure assessment. The Servicer gives
the Borrower information about free and impartial debt advice, including the Money and Pensions Service's debt
advice services, in every arrears letter.

*Statutory notices.* Where a Borrower has missed two instalments, the Servicer sends a notice of sums in arrears
in the form required by section 86B of the CCA within the period required by that section, accompanied by the
arrears information sheet published by the FCA, and repeats the notice at the intervals required while the
arrears continue. The Servicer also sends the annual statements required by section 77A of the CCA and any
notice of default sums required by section 86C.

*Late arrears.* Accounts which are not brought up to date or placed on an arrangement move to the late arrears
team, whose staff are trained to assess income and expenditure using a standard financial statement and to
identify Borrowers who are vulnerable. The team may offer the forbearance options described below, may refer the
Borrower to a debt advice charity with the Borrower's consent, and, where an arrangement cannot be agreed,
explains to the Borrower the options of voluntary termination and voluntary surrender of the vehicle.

*Default notice and termination.* Where the arrears remain unresolved and no arrangement is in place, the
Servicer may serve a default notice under section 87 of the CCA, in the form required by the Consumer Credit
(Enforcement, Default and Termination Notices) Regulations 1983, giving the Borrower not less than 14 days after
service to remedy the breach. If the breach is not remedied, the Servicer may terminate the Finance Agreement
and demand the balance due, subject to the restrictions described under "Repossession and sale of vehicles".
The decision to serve a default notice and to terminate is taken by an arrears manager after a review of the
account, and is not taken where the Borrower is engaging with the Servicer on an affordable arrangement or
where the Servicer has identified a vulnerability which requires a different approach.

*After a default.* Once a Loan is in default as set out in section 11, all amounts received in respect of it are
Recoveries, whether or not a default notice has been served or the Finance Agreement has been terminated. The
Servicer Report shows, for each such Loan, the balance at the date of default and that date. A Loan stays in
default if the Borrower later clears the arrears.

## Forbearance and the Consumer Duty

The Servicer offers forbearance in accordance with the FCA's rules on customers in arrears or default in CONC 7,
which since 2024 incorporate the tailored support for customers in financial difficulty previously set out in
guidance, and with the Consumer Duty. The forbearance options available include:

(a) an arrangement to pay the arrears over a period in addition to the contractual instalment;

(b) a temporary reduction of the monthly payment to an amount the Borrower can afford, for an agreed period
after which the arrangement is reviewed;

(c) a short deferral of instalments where the Borrower's difficulty is expected to be temporary, for example
pending a return to work;

(d) the suspension, reduction or waiver of interest or charges, where this is appropriate to the Borrower's
circumstances;

(e) an extension of the term of the Finance Agreement, within the limit described under "Appointment of the
Servicer"; and

(f) the acceptance of a token payment or a period of no payment where the Borrower's circumstances, including a
serious illness or bereavement, make any payment inappropriate.

The option chosen depends on an assessment of the Borrower's income and expenditure, and the Servicer must not
pressure the Borrower to pay more than the Borrower can sustainably afford. A Loan under a forbearance arrangement
continues to be measured for arrears by reference to its contractual instalments unless its terms have been
varied, and the Servicer Report states the number and balance of Loans subject to each type of arrangement.
Forbearance may reduce or delay the collections available to the Issuer, as described in section 2, but the
Issuer may not direct the Servicer to withhold forbearance which the law or the FCA requires.

## Repossession and sale of vehicles

The vehicle financed under each HP Agreement or PCP Agreement remains the property of the Seller (held on the
Vehicle Trust for the Issuer, see section 12) until the Borrower exercises the option to purchase. After
termination of a Finance Agreement the Servicer may recover the vehicle, subject to the restrictions of the CCA.
Where the Borrower has paid at least one-third of the total price of the vehicle, the vehicle is protected goods
under section 90 of the CCA and may be recovered only with the Borrower's consent or under a court order; the
Servicer may not in any case enter premises to recover a vehicle without a court order under section 92 of the
CCA. In practice, most vehicles are recovered through voluntary surrender by the Borrower or under a return of
goods order or a time order made by the county court (or the sheriff court in Scotland), which may allow the
Borrower further time to pay. Where a vehicle cannot be located, the Servicer uses tracing agents and the
industry vehicle finance register, and makes a claim against any insurer or dealer liable for the loss.

Recovered and returned vehicles are collected by the Servicer's appointed collection agents, inspected and
graded, and sold through national vehicle auction houses or online trade auctions, with reserve prices set by the
Servicer's remarketing team by reference to current trade valuations, the condition of the vehicle and recent
sale prices. The Servicer may sell a vehicle to a dealer where that is expected to achieve a better price than
auction. The net sale proceeds, after the costs of collection, storage, preparation and sale, are applied to the
amount owed by the Borrower, and the Borrower is notified of the sale price and any remaining balance. Where the
proceeds exceed the amount owed, the surplus is repaid to the Borrower.

## Voluntary terminations and PCP maturities

A Borrower may terminate a Finance Agreement at any time before the final payment falls due by giving notice
under section 99 of the CCA and returning the vehicle. On a voluntary termination, the Borrower's liability is
limited under section 100 of the CCA to any arrears, the amount (if any) needed to bring total payments up to
one-half of the total amount payable under the agreement, and compensation for damage to the vehicle beyond fair
wear and tear. The Servicer sells the returned vehicle in the manner described above. The proceeds of sale,
together with amounts paid by the Borrower, are principal received and interest collections of the Issuer; a
Loan which has been voluntarily terminated ceases to form part of the Pool Balance when the vehicle has been sold
and the termination has been settled, and any shortfall between the outstanding principal and those amounts is a
loss borne by the Issuer (a "Voluntary Termination Loss"). The Servicer informs Borrowers of their right to
voluntary termination where required by CONC 7 and does not discourage its exercise.

At the end of a PCP Agreement, the Servicer writes to the Borrower in advance of the Balloon Payment date to
explain the options of paying the Balloon Payment, returning the vehicle or part-exchanging it. Where the
vehicle is returned, it is inspected against the mileage and condition terms of the agreement, any excess mileage
and damage charges are invoiced to the Borrower, and the vehicle is sold. Any amount by which the net sale
proceeds and those charges fall short of the Balloon Payment is a loss borne by the Issuer (a "Residual Value
Loss"). The Servicer reports Voluntary Termination Losses and Residual Value Losses separately from Defaulted
Loans and Recoveries. The Servicer may offer to refinance the Balloon Payment under a new agreement; a new
agreement is not part of the Portfolio, and the Issuer receives the Balloon Payment in full from the refinancing.

## Recoveries and write-off

After a Loan has become a Defaulted Loan, the recoveries team pursues the outstanding balance through the sale of
the vehicle, payment arrangements with the Borrower, claims against insurers and dealers and, where appropriate
and proportionate, court proceedings. The Servicer does not sell Defaulted Loans to debt purchasers without the
consent of the Issuer and the Trustee. The Servicer will write off a Defaulted Loan when, in accordance with the
Credit and Collection Policies, it considers that no further amounts are likely to be recovered at a
reasonable cost, and in any case on the death of a Borrower whose estate is insolvent or on the Borrower's
bankruptcy or sequestration once the Servicer has submitted its claim. Amounts received after a write-off remain
Recoveries. The Recoveries which the Servicer expects for the Transaction are described in section 11; actual
Recoveries depend on the condition of the vehicles, used car prices and the timing of sales and court
proceedings, and may be lower or later than expected.

## Servicer reporting

The Servicer will deliver to the Issuer, the Cash Manager and the Trustee, no later than the fifth Business Day
of each month, a report for the Collection Period ending on the last day of the previous month (the "Servicer
Report"). The Servicer Report sets out, among other things, the interest collections, principal received from
instalments and Balloon Payments, prepayments and Recoveries received during the Collection Period; the Pool
Balance at the start and the end of the Collection Period; the number and balance of Loans in each arrears band;
the Loans which became Defaulted Loans and their defaulted balances; Voluntary Termination Losses and Residual
Value Losses; the Loans subject to forbearance arrangements; any Loans repurchased and the Repurchase Prices
received; any deemed collections and Direct Debit indemnity claims; and the data needed by the Cash Manager to
calculate the cumulative default ratio and the 90+ day arrears ratio defined in section 11. With each Servicer
Report the Servicer delivers loan-level data on each Loan in a form which enables the Issuer to meet its
reporting obligations described in section 18. The Cash Manager uses the Servicer Report to prepare the
calculations described in section 14 and the investor report described in section 16.

The Servicer will also provide to the Issuer and the Trustee, within 120 days after the end of each financial
year, a certificate signed by a director confirming that it has complied with the Servicing Agreement, and will
give them access, on reasonable notice, to the Loan Files and its systems for the purpose of audits. The
Servicer must notify the Issuer and the Trustee promptly of any Servicer Termination Event or of any event which
would, with the giving of notice or the passing of time, become one.

## Servicer Termination Events

Each of the following is a "Servicer Termination Event":

(a) the Servicer fails to pay into the Issuer Transaction Account any amount due from it within three Business
Days after its due date, unless the failure results from a technical error which is remedied within a further
two Business Days;

(b) the Servicer fails to deliver a Servicer Report within five Business Days after its due date;

(c) the Servicer fails to perform any other obligation under the Servicing Agreement, and the failure has a
material adverse effect on the interests of the Noteholders and, if capable of remedy, is not remedied within
30 days after notice from the Issuer or the Trustee;

(d) an Insolvency Event occurs in relation to the Servicer;

(e) the Servicer ceases to hold any authorisation under FSMA required to service the Loans, or the FCA imposes a
requirement or restriction which prevents the Servicer from performing its services; or

(f) the Servicer ceases to carry on all or substantially all of its business of servicing motor finance
agreements.

On a Servicer Termination Event, the Issuer (with the consent of the Trustee) or the Trustee may terminate the
appointment of the Servicer by notice. The Servicer may also resign on not less than six months' notice. No
termination or resignation takes effect until a successor servicer, which holds the authorisations required
under FSMA and has experience of servicing UK motor finance agreements, has been appointed on terms
substantially similar to the Servicing Agreement and the Rating Agencies have been notified. On termination, the
Servicer must transfer the Loan Files, the data on its systems relating to the Loans and the collections it holds
to the successor servicer and must co-operate in the transfer of the servicing, including the redirection of
Direct Debits under the Bacs scheme rules.

## The Back-up Servicer Facilitator

Pellam Corporate Services Ltd (the "Back-up Servicer Facilitator") has agreed in the Servicing Agreement that,
if the appointment of the Servicer is terminated, it will use reasonable endeavours to identify, on behalf of
the Issuer, a suitably authorised and experienced successor servicer and to assist the Issuer in negotiating the
terms of its appointment, within 60 days after the termination notice. The Back-up Servicer Facilitator does not
itself act as servicer and has no obligation to service the Loans, to fund any amount or to guarantee that a
successor servicer will be found. To assist a transfer, the Servicer must maintain an up-to-date description of
its servicing procedures and systems, deliver to the Back-up Servicer Facilitator each month a copy of the
loan-level data and give it access to its systems once a year for a review of the data. The fee of a successor
servicer may be higher than the fee of the Servicer and would rank among the senior fees in the Priority of
Payments described in section 15. A change of servicer may lead to a temporary disruption of collections and
a rise in arrears, as described in section 2.

\newpage

# 14. Cash management and the issuer accounts

## The Cash Management Agreement

Under a cash management agreement dated the Closing Date between Larkmead Bank plc (the "Cash
Manager"), the Issuer, the Servicer and the Trustee (the "Cash Management Agreement"), the Cash Manager will operate the
Issuer Accounts, maintain the Ledgers, calculate on each Determination Date the Available Funds and the amounts
payable on the following Payment Date, and instruct the payments to be made in accordance with the Priority of
Payments set out in section 15. The Cash Manager acts as agent of the Issuer and, after the delivery of an
enforcement notice, of the Trustee. It performs its services on the basis of the Servicer Report and the other
information delivered to it, and is not responsible for verifying that information, except that it must notify
the Issuer and the Trustee if the information is inconsistent on its face.

Larkmead Bank plc also acts as account bank of the Issuer (in this capacity, the "Account Bank") under a
bank account agreement dated the Closing Date between the Issuer, the Account Bank, the Cash Manager and
the Trustee (the "Bank Account Agreement"), and as agent bank and principal paying agent under the agency
agreement described in section 5. The fees of the Cash Manager, the Account Bank, the agent bank and the
principal paying agent, and of the Back-up Servicer Facilitator, are paid by Kestrel Motor Finance Ltd ("Kestrel")
out of the servicer fee and are not separate items of the Priority of Payments described in section 15.

The Cash Manager may resign on not less than three months' notice, and the Issuer (with the consent of the
Trustee) may terminate its appointment if it fails to perform its obligations or becomes subject to an
Insolvency Event. No resignation or termination takes effect until a successor cash manager has been
appointed and the Rating Agencies have been notified.

## The Collection Account and the declaration of trust

Borrowers pay their instalments and other amounts into the Collection Account described in section 13, which is
held by Kestrel with Larkmead Bank plc (the "Collection Account Bank") and also receives payments under Finance
Agreements which do not form part of the Portfolio. Under a declaration of trust made by Kestrel on or before the
Closing Date (the "Collection Account Declaration of Trust"), Kestrel holds all amounts standing to the credit
of the Collection Account on trust for the Issuer, as to the amounts which derive from the Loans, and for the
other beneficiaries and itself, as to the remainder. The Collection Account Declaration of Trust is intended to
ensure that collections on the Loans held in the Collection Account do not form part of Kestrel's estate on its
insolvency. The Collection Account Bank has acknowledged the trust and has waived any right of set-off or
combination against the Collection Account in respect of amounts owed to it by Kestrel.

The Servicer identifies, using the payment references and its loan management platform, the amounts received
in the Collection Account in respect of each Loan, and records them as collections of the Issuer. Collections
which cannot be identified on the day of receipt are held in a suspense account of the Servicer and allocated
within five Business Days. Amounts which are received in the Collection Account in error, and Direct Debit
indemnity claims which reverse collections already transferred to the Issuer, are reconciled by the Servicer
and deducted from the next transfer to the Issuer, with details given in the Servicer Report.

## Sweeping of collections

On each Business Day the Servicer transfers from the Collection Account to the Issuer Transaction Account all
collections on the Loans identified on the previous Business Day, including instalments, Balloon Payments,
settlement amounts, the proceeds of vehicles sold after repossession, surrender, voluntary termination or
return, insurance proceeds and any other Recoveries. The Seller pays into the Issuer Transaction Account any
Repurchase Price and any indemnity amount under the Sale Agreement, and any amount which is treated as a deemed
collection because a Borrower has exercised a right of set-off against Kestrel or because the amount owed by a
Borrower has been reduced by way of redress, on or before the Business Day before the next Payment Date. The
daily sweep limits to one or two Business Days' collections the amount held by Kestrel for the Issuer at any
time; the commingling risk which remains is described in section 2.

If the Collection Account Bank ceases to have the Account Bank Required Ratings, Kestrel must, within 30
calendar days, open a replacement collection account with a bank which has them, transfer the Collection
Account Declaration of Trust to it and instruct Borrowers paying by Direct Debit, card or standing order to pay
into the replacement account. After a Perfection Event, Borrowers will be instructed to pay directly into an
account of the Issuer (see section 12).

## The Issuer Accounts

The Issuer maintains with the Account Bank the following accounts (together, the "Issuer Accounts"):

(a) a transaction account (the "Issuer Transaction Account"), into which all collections, Repurchase Prices,
deemed collections and other amounts received by the Issuer in respect of the Loans are paid, from which
payments are made on each Payment Date in accordance with the Priority of Payments, and into which the
proceeds of the issue of the Notes were paid on the Closing Date before being applied to the Initial Purchase
Price; and

(b) a reserve account (the "Reserve Account"), in which the reserve fund described in section 16 (the
"Reserve Fund") is held.

The Issuer also maintains an account with a bank in the United Kingdom for its share capital and the profits
retained by it, which does not form part of the Issuer Accounts and from which the Issuer pays its corporation
tax and other corporate costs. Each Issuer Account is subject to the security created by the Issuer in favour of
the Trustee under the deed of charge dated the Closing Date (the "Deed of Charge"), and notice of that security
has been given to the Account Bank, which has acknowledged it and waived any right of set-off,
combination or counterclaim against the Issuer Accounts.

No withdrawal may be made from the Issuer Accounts except on the instructions of the Cash Manager in accordance
with the Cash Management Agreement. Between Payment Dates, the Cash Manager may make withdrawals only to pay
amounts received in error, to make payments due to third parties which the Issuer is obliged to make before the
next Payment Date and which are permitted by the Transaction Documents, and to make Authorised Investments.

## The Reserve Account

On the Closing Date, Kestrel will fund the Reserve Fund by a payment into the Reserve Account of the amount
described in section 16. On each Payment Date the Cash Manager transfers from the Reserve Account to the Issuer
Transaction Account the amount (if any) by which the Available Funds are insufficient to pay in full the senior
fees and the interest on each class of Notes, that is, items 1 to 5 of the Priority of Payments described in
section 15, and, after the application of the Available Funds, credits to the Reserve Account the amount paid
under item 6 to restore the Reserve Fund to its required amount. The Reserve Fund is not used to pay principal
or Deferred Consideration. The required amount of the Reserve Fund, its release on the redemption of the Notes
and the trigger event relating to it are described in section 16.

## Ledgers

The Cash Manager maintains the following ledgers (the "Ledgers") in its records for the Issuer:

(a) the "Interest Collections Ledger", to which it credits all interest collections, including the interest
element of instalments, settlement amounts and Repurchase Prices, fees and charges paid by Borrowers and the
interest and investment income earned on the Issuer Accounts;

(b) the "Principal Collections Ledger", to which it credits all principal received from instalments, Balloon
Payments, prepayments and settlement amounts, and the principal element of Repurchase Prices and indemnity
amounts;

(c) the "Recoveries Ledger", to which it credits all Recoveries received in respect of Defaulted Loans;

(d) the "Reserve Fund Ledger", which records the balance of the Reserve Fund, each drawing made under the
previous paragraph and each amount credited to restore it; and

(e) the "Principal Deficiency Ledger", which records the principal deficiency described in section 15 on each
Payment Date, that is, the amount by which the total balance of the Notes exceeds the Pool Balance after the
application of the Available Funds, and its reduction on later Payment Dates.

Each Ledger is debited with the amounts applied from it on each Payment Date. The Ledgers are maintained for
the purposes of calculation and reporting only; the amounts credited to the Interest Collections Ledger, the
Principal Collections Ledger and the Recoveries Ledger are applied together as Available Funds, and the
classification of a receipt as interest or principal does not affect its application.

## Account bank ratings and replacement

The Account Bank must at all times have the minimum short-term and long-term ratings required by each
Rating Agency for an account bank in a transaction with the ratings of the Notes, as set out in the Bank
Account Agreement (the "Account Bank Required Ratings"). If the Account Bank ceases to have the Account
Bank Required Ratings, it must notify the Issuer, the Cash Manager and the Trustee, and the Issuer (or the Cash
Manager on its behalf) must, within 30 calendar days after the downgrade, either:

(a) transfer the Issuer Accounts, and the balances standing to their credit, to a bank which has the Account
Bank Required Ratings and which accedes to the Bank Account Agreement and the Deed of Charge; or

(b) obtain an unconditional and irrevocable guarantee of the obligations of the Account Bank from a
financial institution which has the Account Bank Required Ratings,

or take any other action which the Rating Agencies confirm will not result in the downgrade of the Notes. The
costs of the replacement are borne by the outgoing Account Bank. The Account Bank may also resign
on not less than 60 days' notice, and its appointment may be terminated by the Issuer, with the consent of the
Trustee, on an Insolvency Event, but no resignation or termination takes effect until a replacement account
bank has been appointed and the balances transferred. A replacement may take time to complete, and the Issuer's
cash may be exposed to the credit risk of a downgraded bank during that period, as described in section 2.

## Investment of funds

The Cash Manager may, on behalf of the Issuer, invest the balances standing to the credit of the Issuer
Transaction Account and the Reserve Account between Payment Dates in authorised investments ("Authorised
Investments"), which are:

(a) sterling demand or time deposits with a bank which has the Account Bank Required Ratings; and

(b) sterling money market funds which have the highest rating of each Rating Agency which rates them,

in each case which mature, or can be redeemed without penalty, on or before the Business Day before the next
Determination Date, which are not securitisation positions and which are held in the name of the Issuer subject
to the security of the Deed of Charge. The Cash Manager will not invest in any derivative instrument and will not
make an investment which would expose the Issuer to a currency or a market risk. The income from Authorised
Investments is credited to the Interest Collections Ledger. Any loss on an Authorised Investment is borne by the
Issuer, and the Cash Manager is not liable for it unless it results from its negligence, wilful default or
fraud. The Cash Manager expects to hold the balances of the Issuer Accounts as deposits with the Issuer Account
Bank.

## Calculation and determination of Available Funds

On the fourth Business Day before each Payment Date (each a "Determination Date") the Cash Manager will calculate,
on the basis of the Servicer Report for the Collection Period ending before that Payment Date:

(a) the Available Funds for the Collection Period, being the interest collections, principal received from
instalments (including Balloon Payments), prepayments and Recoveries received during the Collection Period, as
defined in section 11, together with any deemed collections, Repurchase Prices and indemnity amounts received in
respect of that Collection Period and the income credited to the Interest Collections Ledger;

(b) the Pool Balance at the end of the Collection Period, the defaulted balances of the Loans which became
Defaulted Loans during it, the cumulative default ratio and the 90+ day arrears ratio, each as defined in
section 11;

(c) the amount of each item payable under the Priority of Payments described in section 15, including the senior
fees, the interest payable on each class of Notes for the interest period ending on the Payment Date at the rate
determined by the agent bank in accordance with the Conditions in section 5, the amount required to restore the
Reserve Fund and the principal payable on the Notes;

(d) any drawing on the Reserve Fund required under section 16;

(e) whether a trigger event described in section 16 has occurred, and consequently whether principal is to be
paid pro rata or sequentially as described in section 4; and

(f) the balance of the Principal Deficiency Ledger after the application of the Available Funds, and the
Deferred Consideration payable to Kestrel.

The Cash Manager notifies the results to the Issuer, the Trustee, the Servicer and the principal paying agent
no later than the Business Day after the Determination Date, and on the Payment Date instructs the Issuer Account
Bank to make the payments from the Issuer Transaction Account and the Reserve Account. The amounts of interest
and principal to be paid on each class of Notes are notified to the Noteholders in accordance with the
Conditions and are shown in the investor report described in section 16. Each determination of the Cash Manager
is, in the absence of manifest error, final and binding on the Issuer, the Trustee and the Noteholders.

If the Servicer Report for a Collection Period has not been received by the Determination Date, the Cash
Manager will estimate the Available Funds by reference to the amounts received in the Issuer Transaction Account
during the Collection Period and, for the allocation between interest and principal, the average of the three
most recent Servicer Reports. Where the Cash Manager makes such an estimate, it will make no payment of principal
beyond the amount which it is reasonably satisfied is available and will pay no Deferred Consideration on that
Payment Date. Any difference between the estimated and the actual amounts is reconciled on the next Payment Date
for which a Servicer Report is received, by adjusting the Available Funds for that Payment Date. The failure of
the Servicer to deliver a Servicer Report may be a Servicer Termination Event (see section 13).

\newpage

# 15. Priority of payments
On each payment date the issuer applies the available funds in the following order. An item is paid only
when every item above it has been paid in full.

1. **Senior fees**: the servicer fee of 1.00% per year on the pool balance at the
   start of the period (paid monthly at one-twelfth of that rate), and the trustee fee of
   £2,500.00 per payment date.
2. **Class A interest.**
3. **Class B interest.**
4. **Class C interest.**
5. **Class D interest.**
6. **Reserve fund top-up**: the amount needed to bring the reserve fund back to its required amount.
7. **Principal on the notes**: the amount needed to reduce the total note balance to the pool balance at the
   end of the period, paid pro rata or sequentially as described in section 4.
8. **Deferred consideration**: any remaining amount is paid to the originator.

If the available funds are not enough to pay items 1 to 5 in full, the shortfall is drawn from the reserve
fund. The reserve fund is never used to pay principal or deferred consideration.

If the funds left for item 7 are lower than the amount needed, the total note balance stays above the pool
balance. The difference is called a *principal deficiency*; it is reduced on later payment dates from the
funds left after item 6.

\newpage

# 16. Reserve fund and trigger events
**Reserve fund.** On the closing date the originator funds a reserve fund of
£1,111,137.93, equal to
1.50% of the initial balance of the notes. The
required amount stays at this level for the life of the transaction.

**Trigger events.** A trigger event occurs when any of the following tests fails. The tests use the figures at
the end of each period and are reported in each investor report.

*Test 1 — Cumulative default ratio.* The cumulative default ratio must not exceed:

| Periods | Maximum cumulative default ratio |
|---|---:|
| Periods 1 to 6 | 1.00% |
| Periods 7 to 12 | 2.00% |

*Test 2 — 90+ day arrears ratio.* The 90+ day arrears ratio must not exceed 1.25%.

*Test 3 — Reserve fund.* After the previous payment date, the reserve fund must be at its required amount.

**Consequence.** From the payment date that follows the period in which a trigger event occurs, principal is
paid sequentially (section 4). The switch is permanent, even if the test is passed again in a later period.

**Reporting.** Within 15 days after each payment date, the servicer publishes an investor report showing the
pool performance, the arrears, the application of funds, the note balances, the reserve fund and the result
of each test in this section.

\newpage

# 17. Weighted average lives of the notes

The weighted average life of a note is the average time, in years, from the closing date until each pound of
its principal is repaid, weighted by the principal repaid. It depends on the rate at which borrowers repay
their loans early, which cannot be predicted. The tables below are calculated on the following assumptions
(the "Modelling Assumptions"):

(a) the loans pay their scheduled instalments, and each PCP loan pays its balloon payment on its last
instalment date;

(b) the loans prepay at the constant annual rate shown (the "Constant Prepayment Rate"), applied monthly;

(c) no loan defaults, is in arrears or is repurchased, and no trigger event occurs, so that principal is paid
pro rata to all classes as described in section 4;

(d) the first payment date is 20 February 2026 and payments are made on the 20th day of each
month;

(e) where stated, the issuer exercises the clean-up call described in Condition 6 on the first payment date on
which the pool balance is less than 10% of the pool balance at the cut-off date.

Because principal is paid pro rata until a trigger event occurs, every class of notes has the same weighted
average life under the Modelling Assumptions. The actual weighted average lives will differ, and the
differences may be material.

**Table 17.1 — Weighted average lives of the Class A, B, C and D Notes**

| Constant prepayment rate | Weighted average life (years), with clean-up call | Last payment date, with call | Weighted average life (years), to maturity | Last payment date, to maturity |
|---:|---:|---|---:|---|
| 0% | 1.68 | February 2029 | 1.72 | October 2030 |
| 5% | 1.54 | December 2028 | 1.58 | October 2030 |
| 10% | 1.41 | September 2028 | 1.46 | October 2030 |
| 15% | 1.30 | July 2028 | 1.34 | October 2030 |
| 20% | 1.20 | May 2028 | 1.24 | October 2030 |
| 25% | 1.11 | April 2028 | 1.14 | October 2030 |
| 30% | 1.02 | February 2028 | 1.06 | October 2030 |

**Table 17.2 — Scheduled amortisation of the pool**

| Payment date | Scheduled pool balance (no prepayments, no defaults) | Pool factor |
|---|---:|---:|
| 20 February 2026 | £72,557,041.27 | 0.9795 |
| 20 May 2026 | £67,929,598.69 | 0.9170 |
| 20 August 2026 | £62,173,138.72 | 0.8393 |
| 20 November 2026 | £56,200,045.26 | 0.7587 |
| 20 February 2027 | £50,486,509.61 | 0.6816 |
| 20 May 2027 | £44,713,568.97 | 0.6036 |
