# 18. Regulatory disclosures

## The UK securitisation framework

The securitisation of the Loans by the Issuer is a "securitisation" within the meaning of the Securitisation
Regulations 2024 (SI 2024/102) (the "Securitisation Regulations"), made by HM Treasury under the Financial
Services and Markets Act 2000 ("FSMA 2000"). Since 1 November 2024 the Securitisation Regulations, together
with the rules made under them by the FCA in the securitisation sourcebook of the FCA Handbook ("SECN") and by
the PRA in the Securitisation Part of the PRA Rulebook (the "PRA Securitisation Rules"), have replaced the
version of Regulation (EU) 2017/2402 that formed part of domestic law by virtue of the EUWA. In this
Prospectus the Securitisation Regulations, SECN and the PRA Securitisation Rules, together with any guidance,
templates and statements of policy published by the FCA or the PRA under them, are referred to as the "UK
Securitisation Framework". The FCA rules apply to originators, original lenders, sponsors and securitisation
special purpose entities ("SSPEs") that are not PRA-authorised persons, and to institutional investors that
are FCA-regulated; the PRA rules apply to PRA-authorised persons acting in those capacities. The Originator is
authorised by the FCA and is not a PRA-authorised person, and the Issuer is an SSPE; their obligations as
originator and SSPE are therefore set out in SECN.

The UK Securitisation Framework imposes obligations on three groups of persons: on the originator, the sponsor,
the original lender and the SSPE (together, the "Sell-Side Parties"), principally in relation to risk
retention, transparency and credit-granting; on institutional investors, in relation to due diligence before
and while they hold a securitisation position; and on any person who wishes a securitisation to be treated as
a simple, transparent and standardised securitisation. This section describes how the Transaction is intended
to address each of these obligations. It does not purport to be a complete description of the UK
Securitisation Framework, and it is not legal or regulatory advice. Each prospective investor is responsible
for determining, on the basis of its own analysis and with its own advisers, whether the information in this
Prospectus and the information that will be made available after the Closing Date is sufficient to allow it
to comply with the requirements that apply to it.

The UK Securitisation Framework is subject to review. HM Treasury, the FCA and the PRA have proposed
amendments to it which, among other things, would revise the transparency templates, remove the requirement
for public securitisations to report to a securitisation repository, simplify the due diligence obligations of
institutional investors and add further options to the risk retention rules. Those proposals may be adopted in
a form that differs from the form proposed, and they may take effect on dates that are not yet known. The
Originator and the Issuer will comply with the UK Securitisation Framework as it applies to the Transaction
from time to time, and the Originator may change the form, the content or the channel of the reports described
in this section to the extent that an amendment to the UK Securitisation Framework permits or requires it,
without the consent of the Noteholders. None of the Issuer, the Originator, the Arranger, the Lead Manager and
the Trustee gives any assurance as to the content, the timing or the effect of any amendment.

## Risk retention

Kestrel Motor Finance Ltd (the "Retention Holder") will undertake to the Issuer and the Trustee in the
Subscription Agreement and in the Servicing Agreement (the "Retention Undertaking") that, for so long as any
Note is outstanding, it will retain on an ongoing basis a material net economic interest in the securitisation
of not less than 5% of the nominal value of each class of Notes, in accordance with SECN 5, by holding a
*vertical slice*, as described in section 1 (*Transaction overview*). On the Closing Date the Retention Holder
will subscribe for not less than 5% of the principal amount of each of the Class A Notes, the Class B Notes,
the Class C Notes and the Class D Notes (the "Retained Notes"). Because principal is applied to every class of
Notes in the manner described in section 4 and section 15, and the Retained Notes rank *pari passu* with the
other Notes of the same class, the Retained Notes will continue to represent not less than 5% of the principal
amount outstanding of each class throughout the life of the Transaction.

Under the Retention Undertaking the Retention Holder will also undertake that:

(a) it will not sell, transfer or otherwise surrender all or part of the rights, benefits or obligations
arising from the Retained Notes, and will not subject them to any credit risk mitigation, short position or
other hedge, except to the extent permitted by SECN 5;

(b) it will not change the method by which it retains its net economic interest, except in the circumstances,
and in the manner, permitted by SECN 5;

(c) it will confirm in each Investor Report (as defined below) that it continues to hold the Retained Notes,
and it will notify the Cash Manager and the Trustee promptly if it ceases to do so; and

(d) it will provide to the Issuer, the Trustee and the Noteholders such further information as is reasonably
required to enable an institutional investor to verify compliance with the Retention Undertaking, subject to
any applicable law on confidentiality and data protection.

The Retention Holder is the originator of the Loans for the purposes of the UK Securitisation Framework. It
originated every Loan in its own name under the Finance Agreements described in section 9. It has not been
established, and does not operate, for the sole purpose of securitising exposures: it is an established motor
finance company with a strategy and a capacity to meet payment obligations consistent with a broader business,
and its principal source of revenue is the income from the motor finance agreements that it originates and
holds or funds, not the sale of exposures to securitisations. The Retention Holder has not selected the Loans
to be sold to the Issuer with the aim of rendering losses on the Loans higher than the losses over the same
period on comparable exposures held on its own balance sheet. Each Loan was selected from the Retention
Holder's portfolio of loans that satisfied the eligibility criteria in section 11 on the cut-off date, without
any further selection by reference to credit quality.

None of the Issuer, the Arranger, the Lead Manager and the Trustee makes any representation that the Retention
Undertaking is sufficient to satisfy the requirements that apply to any investor, and none of them will
monitor compliance by the Retention Holder with the Retention Undertaking; the Trustee will rely on the
confirmations in the Investor Reports without further enquiry.

\newpage

## Transparency and reporting

### Reporting entity

For the purposes of SECN 6, the Originator, the Issuer and the other Sell-Side Parties have designated the
Originator as the entity responsible for fulfilling the transparency requirements of the UK Securitisation
Framework in respect of the Transaction (in that capacity, the "Reporting Entity"). The Reporting Entity has
appointed the Cash Manager under the Cash Management Agreement to prepare, and to make available on its behalf,
the Investor Reports and the loan-level data, and has appointed the Servicer to provide the Cash Manager with
the information on the Loans that it needs for that purpose. The appointment of the Cash Manager does not
relieve the Reporting Entity of its responsibility for compliance with SECN 6.

### Information made available before pricing

Before pricing, the Originator made available to potential investors, on request:

(a) static and dynamic historical data on the default, arrears, voluntary termination, prepayment and
recovery performance of the hire purchase and personal contract purchase agreements originated by the
Originator, covering a period of not less than five years and broken down by product type, as referred to in
section 8;

(b) a liability cash flow model which precisely represents the contractual relationship between the Loans and
the payments flowing between the Originator, the Issuer, the Noteholders and the other parties to the
Transaction, which the Originator will continue to make available to investors on an ongoing basis and to
potential investors on request;

(c) loan-level information on the Loans in the portfolio at the cut-off date, in the form of the underlying
exposures template for automobile loans and leases set out in SECN 11; and

(d) a draft of this Prospectus and the drafts of the principal Transaction Documents, including the
agreement under which the Seller sells the Loans to the Issuer, the Servicing Agreement, the Cash Management
Agreement, the Trust Deed and the Deed of Charge.

The final Transaction Documents and this Prospectus in final form will be made available within the period
after the Closing Date required by SECN 6.

### Ongoing reporting

On each payment date, the Cash Manager will prepare, on behalf of the Reporting Entity and from the information
provided by the Servicer, a report on the Transaction for the preceding monthly period (each, an "Investor
Report"). Each Investor Report will be made available within 15 days after the payment date to which it
relates, as stated in section 16, and will contain the information required by SECN 6, in the form of the
investor report template for non-asset-backed commercial paper securitisations in SECN 11, together with:

(a) the collections, the available funds and their application in the priority of payments in section 15;

(b) the principal amount outstanding of each class of Notes, the interest rate for each class and the amount
of interest and principal paid;

(c) the balance of the reserve fund and the amounts drawn from and credited to it;

(d) the performance of the Loans, including defaults, arrears by band, voluntary terminations, prepayments,
vehicle recoveries and losses;

(e) the result of each trigger event test in section 16 and whether a trigger event has occurred; and

(f) the confirmation of the Retention Holder described above under *Risk retention*.

At the same time, the Cash Manager will make available on behalf of the Reporting Entity the loan-level
information on the Loans as at the end of the relevant monthly period, in the form of the underlying exposures
template for automobile loans and leases in SECN 11. SECN 6 requires this information and the investor report
to be made available at least quarterly; in respect of the Transaction both will be made available monthly.
Each data submission will use the same reporting date as the Investor Report to which it relates, and will
identify the Loans by a unique identifier that does not allow a borrower to be identified directly.

### Inside information and significant events

Without delay, the Reporting Entity will make available any inside information relating to the Transaction
which it is obliged to make public under Regulation (EU) No 596/2014 as it forms part of domestic law by
virtue of the EUWA, to the extent that regulation applies, and any significant event that SECN 6 requires to
be disclosed. A significant event includes a material breach of the obligations in the Transaction Documents,
a change in the structural features of the Transaction that can materially affect its performance, a change
in the risk characteristics of the Transaction or of the Loans, the occurrence of a trigger event described
in section 16, the replacement of the Servicer, the Cash Manager or the Account Bank, and any material
amendment to the Transaction Documents. Each such notification will also be made to the Noteholders in
accordance with the notice provisions of the Conditions.

### Public or private securitisation, and the securitisation repository

The Notes are not, and will not be, admitted to trading on any trading venue, and no prospectus is required to
be published in respect of them under the UK prospectus regime. The Transaction is therefore expected to be a
private securitisation for the purposes of the UK Securitisation Framework, even though the Issuer has
prepared this Prospectus for the information of investors. The requirement in SECN 6 that the information
on a public securitisation be made available by means of a securitisation repository registered with the FCA
therefore does not apply to the Transaction. Instead, the Reporting Entity will make the information described
in this section available to the Noteholders, to the FCA and, on request, to potential investors, by means of
a secure website maintained by the Cash Manager, access to which will be given to each Noteholder and to each
potential investor that confirms its status as a professional investor. The Reporting Entity will also submit
to the FCA the notification required for private securitisations, in the form and within the period required
by the UK Securitisation Framework, and will update it when the UK Securitisation Framework requires.

If, as a result of a change in law or in the regulatory treatment of the Transaction, the Transaction becomes
subject to a requirement to report through a securitisation repository, the Reporting Entity will appoint a
securitisation repository registered with the FCA and the Issuer will bear its costs as an expense of the
Issuer. Conversely, if the requirement for public securitisations to report to a securitisation repository is
removed, as has been proposed, this will not affect the obligation of the Reporting Entity to make the
information available on the website described above. The website and its contents do not form part of this Prospectus.

\newpage

## Credit-granting criteria

SECN 8 requires originators, sponsors and original lenders to apply to exposures to be securitised the same
sound and well-defined criteria for credit-granting that they apply to non-securitised exposures, to apply
the same clearly established processes for approving and, where relevant, amending, renewing and refinancing
credits, and to have effective systems in place to apply those criteria and processes. Where the underlying
exposures are consumer credit agreements, the originator must also have assessed the creditworthiness of the
borrower on the basis required by the rules that apply to the originator.

The Originator has confirmed to the Issuer that each Loan was originated in the ordinary course of its business
under the underwriting criteria and processes described in section 9, which are the same criteria and
processes that it applies to motor finance agreements which it does not securitise; that it has effective
systems to apply those criteria and processes; and that, before entering into each Loan, it carried out the
creditworthiness and affordability assessment required by CONC 5.2A, as described in section 9. The
Originator has also confirmed that no Loan was marketed and underwritten on the premise that the applicant or,
where applicable, an intermediary was made aware that the information provided by the applicant might not be
verified by the Originator. The Originator will disclose in the Investor Reports any material change to its
underwriting criteria made after the Closing Date which applies to its new lending, to the extent SECN
requires such disclosure; such a change will not affect the Loans, which were originated before the cut-off
date.

The Loans are not, and will not include, any securitisation position, so the Transaction is not a
resecuritisation. No Loan was in arrears on the cut-off date, as required by the eligibility criteria in
section 11, and the Originator has confirmed that, to the best of its knowledge, no Loan was owed on the
cut-off date by a borrower who had been declared insolvent or had entered into a debt restructuring process
with the Originator in the three years before the cut-off date.

## Due diligence requirements for institutional investors

### UK institutional investors

Institutional investors that are subject to the UK Securitisation Framework, including credit institutions
and investment firms, insurance and reinsurance undertakings, UK alternative investment fund managers, UK
UCITS management companies and the trustees and managers of occupational pension schemes, must comply with
the due diligence requirements that apply to them before holding a securitisation position and for so long
as they hold it. Those requirements are set out, for FCA-regulated institutional investors, in SECN 4, for
PRA-authorised persons, in the PRA Securitisation Rules and, for the trustees and managers of occupational
pension schemes, in the Securitisation Regulations and the rules made under them.

Before holding a position in the Notes, such an institutional investor must, among other things, verify that
the Originator grants credit on the basis of sound and well-defined criteria and clearly established
processes; verify that the Retention Holder retains a material net economic interest in accordance with the
UK Securitisation Framework and that the retention is disclosed; verify that the information required by
SECN 6 has been made available, or, where the Transaction were not subject to SECN 6, that sufficient
information has been made available to allow it to carry out its assessment; and carry out a due diligence
assessment which enables it to assess the risks involved, including the risk characteristics of the Notes and
of the Loans and all the structural features of the Transaction that can materially affect the performance of
the Notes. While it holds the Notes it must have written procedures to monitor its position and the
performance of the Loans, perform stress tests appropriate to its position, report to its management body on
the risks, and be able to demonstrate to its regulator that it has a comprehensive and thorough understanding
of the Notes and of the Loans and that it has implemented the required policies and procedures. An institutional investor that has
delegated the management of its investments should consider which person is responsible for compliance under
the rules that apply to it.

A failure by an institutional investor to comply with the requirements that apply to it may result in
additional regulatory capital charges or other measures being imposed on it by its regulator. Each of the
Issuer, the Originator, the Arranger, the Lead Manager and the Trustee makes no representation that the
information in this Prospectus, or the information to be made available after the Closing Date, is sufficient
for any investor to comply with its due diligence requirements, and none of them will have any liability to
an investor that fails to do so.

### EEA institutional investors

Institutional investors established in the EEA are subject to the due diligence requirements of Article 5 of
the EU Securitisation Regulation. Where the originator, sponsor or original lender is established outside the
European Union, as the Originator is, Article 5 requires an EEA institutional investor to verify, among other
things, that the originator retains on an ongoing basis a material net economic interest determined in
accordance with Article 6 of the EU Securitisation Regulation, and that the originator, sponsor or SSPE has
made available the information required by Article 7 of the EU Securitisation Regulation in accordance with
the frequency and modalities provided for in that Article.

The Retention Holder intends that the vertical slice described above will also constitute the retention of a
material net economic interest of not less than 5% of the nominal value of each class of Notes within the
meaning of Article 6 of the EU Securitisation Regulation, as in force on the Closing Date, and it does not
intend to change its method of retention after the Closing Date. However, the Retention Holder has given the
Retention Undertaking only by reference to the UK Securitisation Framework; it has not undertaken to comply
with the EU Securitisation Regulation, and it will not be in breach of the Retention Undertaking if the EU and
UK requirements diverge. The information described in this section will be prepared in the form required by
the UK Securitisation Framework and not in the templates adopted under Article 7 of the EU Securitisation
Regulation, and no party has undertaken to make information available through a securitisation repository
registered in the European Union.

Legislative amendments to the EU Securitisation Regulation, including amendments to Article 5 which would
alter the information that an EEA institutional investor must verify in respect of a securitisation whose
originator is established outside the European Union, have been proposed and are in the course of the EU
legislative process; they had not been adopted on the date of this Prospectus. Each EEA institutional
investor should therefore make its own assessment, with its own advisers, of whether the information made
available under the UK Securitisation Framework is sufficient for it to comply with Article 5 of the EU
Securitisation Regulation as in force from time to time. None of the Issuer, the Originator, the Arranger,
the Lead Manager and the Trustee makes any representation in that respect.

\newpage

## Simple, transparent and standardised securitisation

The Transaction has not been, and is not intended to be, designated as a simple, transparent and standardised
securitisation within the meaning of the UK Securitisation Framework ("UK STS"). No notification has been or
will be made to the FCA that the Transaction meets the UK STS requirements, and no third party has been
appointed to verify compliance with them. The Transaction is also not a simple, transparent and standardised
securitisation within the meaning of the EU Securitisation Regulation; that designation is not available to a
securitisation whose originator, sponsor and SSPE are established outside the European Union.

As a result, the Notes will not benefit from the preferential regulatory treatment which certain investors may
apply to UK STS securitisation positions, including the treatment described below under *Regulatory capital
and liquidity treatment*.

## Regulatory capital and liquidity treatment

None of the Issuer, the Originator, the Arranger, the Lead Manager and the Trustee makes any representation to
any prospective investor or purchaser of the Notes as to the regulatory capital treatment of the Notes, as to
whether the Notes qualify as high quality liquid assets for the purposes of the liquidity coverage ratio rules
that apply to an investor, or as to the treatment of the Notes under any other prudential regime, on the
Closing Date or at any time in the future.

Credit institutions and investment firms subject to the prudential requirements of the PRA, and their EEA
equivalents subject to Regulation (EU) No 575/2013 (as amended), should note that the capital treatment of a
securitisation position depends, among other things, on whether the position is a UK STS (or EU STS) position,
on the approach to the calculation of risk-weighted exposure amounts that the investor is permitted to use,
and on the investor's compliance with the due diligence requirements. Because the Transaction is not
designated as UK STS, the Notes are not expected to qualify as Level 2B assets under the liquidity coverage
ratio rules applicable to UK institutions, which require, among other things, that a securitisation position be
a UK STS position, nor under the equivalent EU rules. Insurance and reinsurance undertakings subject to the
UK Solvency II regime (Solvency UK) should consider the spread risk charge applicable to securitisation positions that are
not STS positions. The prudential rules that apply to securitisation positions
in the United Kingdom continue to change, and investors should consult their own advisers on the consequences
for the Notes.

## Bank of England eligibility

The Notes have not been structured with a view to their being eligible as collateral in the Sterling Monetary
Framework of the Bank of England, and no application has been made for that purpose. No representation is
made that any class of Notes will be, or will remain, eligible collateral for any central bank.

\newpage

## United States regulatory matters

### The Volcker Rule

Section 13 of the United States Bank Holding Company Act of 1956, as amended, and its implementing regulations
(together, the "Volcker Rule") generally prohibit "banking entities" (as defined in the Volcker Rule) from
acquiring or retaining an ownership interest in, or sponsoring, a "covered fund", subject to certain
exemptions and exclusions. The Issuer is of the view that it is not, and immediately after the issue of the
Notes and the application of the proceeds will not be, a covered fund. In reaching that view the Issuer
intends to rely on an exemption or exclusion from the definition of "investment company" under the United
States Investment Company Act of 1940, as amended, other than the exemptions in section 3(c)(1) and section
3(c)(7) of that Act, so that the Issuer falls outside the definition of covered fund; other exclusions may also
be available. The Notes are offered only outside the United States to persons that are not U.S. persons in
reliance on Regulation S under the Securities Act, as described in the Important notices and in section 20. No assurance is given that a regulator or a
court would agree with that view, and any prospective investor that is a banking entity should consult its own
advisers.

### United States risk retention

The credit risk retention regulations adopted under Section 15G of the United States Securities Exchange Act
of 1934, as amended (the "U.S. Risk Retention Rules"), generally require the sponsor of a securitisation
transaction to retain not less than 5% of the credit risk of the assets collateralising the issued securities.
The Transaction is not intended to involve the retention by a sponsor of a credit risk interest for the
purposes of the U.S. Risk Retention Rules, and the Originator intends to rely on the exemption for certain
foreign-related transactions in the U.S. Risk Retention Rules. That exemption is available if, among other
conditions, the transaction is not required to be and is not registered under the Securities Act, no more than
10% of the value of all classes of securities issued is sold or transferred to, or for the account or benefit
of, U.S. persons (as defined in the U.S. Risk Retention Rules, "Risk Retention U.S. Persons"), neither the
sponsor nor the issuer is organised under the laws of, or is a branch located in, the United States, and no
more than 25% of the assets were acquired from an affiliate or branch of the sponsor or the issuer organised
or located in the United States. Accordingly, the Notes may not be purchased by Risk Retention U.S. Persons
without the prior written consent of the Originator, and each purchaser of Notes will be deemed to represent
that it is not a Risk Retention U.S. Person. Failure of the Transaction to comply with the U.S. Risk Retention
Rules could adversely affect the market value and the liquidity of the Notes.

## Benchmarks

The rate of interest on each class of Notes is determined by reference to Compounded Daily SONIA, as described
in section 4 and in the Conditions in section 5. SONIA is administered by the Bank of England, which, as a
central bank, falls outside the scope of the UK Benchmarks Regulation, as explained in the Important notices.
The Issuer is not a supervised entity for the purposes of Article 28(2) of the UK Benchmarks Regulation.
The Conditions set out the fallback provisions which apply if SONIA ceases to be provided or if its methodology
changes materially, and the risks associated with the use of SONIA, including the absence of a hedge of the
basis between the fixed rates on the Loans and the floating rate on the Notes, are described in section 2.

## Credit rating agencies

The Issuer has appointed the Rating Agencies, each of which is established in the United Kingdom and registered
under the UK CRA Regulation, to rate the Notes. The UK CRA Regulation requires the issuer or a related third
party that intends to solicit a credit rating of a structured finance instrument to appoint at least two credit
rating agencies, and to consider appointing at least one credit rating agency with a small market share; the
Issuer has complied with those requirements.

\newpage

# 19. Taxation

## General

The following is a general summary of the Issuer's understanding of current United Kingdom tax law as applied
in England and Wales and of the published practice of HM Revenue & Customs ("HMRC"), which may not be binding
on HMRC, relating only to the United Kingdom withholding tax treatment of payments of interest on the Notes,
to United Kingdom stamp duty and stamp duty reserve tax in relation to the Notes, and to the United Kingdom
corporation tax treatment of the Issuer. It does not deal with any other United Kingdom tax aspect of
acquiring, holding or disposing of the Notes, it is not exhaustive, and it is not tax advice. It relates only to
persons who are the absolute beneficial owners of their Notes and of the interest on them, and it may not apply
to certain classes of person, such as dealers in securities, persons connected with the Issuer, persons who
hold the Notes as part of a tax avoidance arrangement or in connection with a trade carried on through a
permanent establishment or branch in the United Kingdom, and persons to whom special rules apply. The law and
practice described below may change, possibly with retrospective effect.

Prospective Noteholders who are in any doubt as to their tax position, or who may be subject to tax in a
jurisdiction other than the United Kingdom, should consult their own professional advisers. The tax treatment
of a Noteholder depends on its individual circumstances, and the tax legislation of the Noteholder's
jurisdiction of residence and of the Issuer's jurisdiction of incorporation may have an effect on the income
received from the Notes.

## United Kingdom withholding tax on interest

### The general rule

The Issuer is incorporated and resident for tax purposes in the United Kingdom, and interest on the Notes will
have a United Kingdom source. Because the Notes may remain outstanding for more than one year, interest on the
Notes is expected to be "yearly interest" for United Kingdom tax purposes. A company that pays yearly interest
with a United Kingdom source is in general obliged to deduct United Kingdom income tax at the basic rate
(currently 20%) from the payment, unless an exemption or relief applies or HMRC has given a direction to the
contrary under an applicable double taxation treaty.

The exemption for "quoted Eurobonds" in section 882 of the Income Tax Act 2007 (the "ITA 2007") applies only
to securities that are, and continue to be, listed on a recognised stock exchange or admitted to trading on a
multilateral trading facility operated by a regulated recognised stock exchange. The Notes are not listed and
will not be admitted to trading on any trading venue, so that exemption is not available. The Issuer will
instead rely, where their conditions are met, on the exemptions described below, as provided in Condition 8(c)
(*United Kingdom withholding*).

### The qualifying private placement exemption

Section 888A of the ITA 2007 and the Income Tax (Qualifying Private Placement) Regulations 2015 (SI 2015/2002)
(together, the "QPP Rules") provide that the duty to deduct income tax does not apply to a payment of interest
on a security that is a "qualifying private placement". In summary, and without describing every condition,
the Issuer may pay interest on a Note without deduction under the QPP Rules where:

(a) the Note is a security that represents a loan relationship to which the Issuer, a company, is party as
debtor;

(b) the Note is not listed on a recognised stock exchange;

(c) the term of the Note does not exceed 50 years, which is satisfied because the final maturity date of the
Notes is 20 December 2032;

(d) the Notes are issued under an arrangement under which the aggregate value of the securities issued is not
less than £10 million;

(e) the person beneficially entitled to the interest is resident in a "qualifying territory", that is, a
territory with which the United Kingdom has a double taxation treaty that contains a non-discrimination
provision, and is not connected with the Issuer;

(f) the person beneficially entitled to the interest holds the Note for genuine commercial reasons and not as
part of a tax advantage scheme, and the Issuer has entered into the arrangements for genuine commercial
reasons and not as part of a tax advantage scheme; and

(g) the Issuer has received from that person a certificate (a "Creditor Certificate") confirming, among other
things, that it is beneficially entitled to the interest for genuine commercial reasons, that it is resident
in a qualifying territory and that it is not connected with the Issuer, and the Issuer has no reason to
believe that any statement in the Creditor Certificate is incorrect.

Because the Notes are held in global registered form through the clearing systems, the Issuer can apply the
exemption only in respect of the interest to which a Noteholder is beneficially entitled and for which a valid
Creditor Certificate has been delivered, through the procedures established by the Principal Paying Agent and
the clearing systems, before the relevant payment date. A Noteholder that is not resident in a qualifying
territory, or that does not deliver a Creditor Certificate, cannot benefit from the QPP Rules and must rely on
another exemption or relief.

### Other exemptions and reliefs

The Issuer may also pay interest on the Notes without deduction where it reasonably believes, at the time of the
payment, that the person beneficially entitled to the interest is a company resident in the United Kingdom, or
a non-resident company that carries on a trade in the United Kingdom through a permanent establishment and must
bring the interest into account in computing its chargeable profits, or another person listed in sections 933
to 937 of the ITA 2007, such as certain partnerships of such companies and certain exempt bodies. If that belief
proves to be incorrect, the Issuer may be liable to account for the tax that it should have deducted, together
with interest and penalties, unless it took reasonable care.

A Noteholder that is resident for tax purposes in a jurisdiction which has a double taxation treaty with the
United Kingdom may be entitled to receive interest without deduction, or subject to deduction at a reduced rate,
if HMRC directs the Issuer accordingly following a claim by that Noteholder in the prescribed form, or may
recover from HMRC some or all of the tax deducted. A claim under a treaty takes time to process, and a direction
does not have retrospective effect for payments already made.

### Consequences of a deduction

If interest on the Notes is paid after the deduction of United Kingdom income tax, the Issuer will not pay any
additional amount to the Noteholders in respect of the amount deducted (Condition 8(b) (*No gross-up*)). A
change in United Kingdom tax law which requires the Issuer to make a deduction from payments on the Notes may
entitle the Issuer to redeem the Notes under Condition 6(e) (*Optional redemption for tax reasons*). Payments of
principal on the Notes, and payments in respect of the Notes which are not interest, may be made without any
deduction for or on account of United Kingdom income tax.

### Provision of information

HMRC has powers to obtain information and documents relating to securities, including information about
payments of interest on the Notes and about the persons for whom, or on whose behalf, those payments are made or
received. Any paying agent or other person in the United Kingdom that pays interest to, or receives interest for
the benefit of, a Noteholder may be required to provide that information to HMRC, and HMRC may exchange it with
the tax authorities of other jurisdictions under international agreements, including the arrangements known as
the Common Reporting Standard implemented in the United Kingdom by the International Tax Compliance
Regulations 2015 (SI 2015/878). Condition 8(d) (*Tax information*) requires each Noteholder to provide the
information reasonably requested for these purposes.

\newpage

## United Kingdom stamp duty and stamp duty reserve tax

No United Kingdom stamp duty or stamp duty reserve tax ("SDRT") should be payable on the issue of the Notes, on
their delivery into the clearing systems, or on a transfer of the Notes or of an interest in them, whether by
book-entry transfer within the clearing systems or by an instrument of transfer of a Note in definitive form.
That is because the Notes are expected to constitute "loan capital" for the purposes of section 78 of the
Finance Act 1986 and to fall within the exemption in section 79(4) of that Act: they do not carry a right of
conversion into shares or other securities, or to the acquisition of shares or other securities; they do not
carry a right to interest the amount of which exceeds a reasonable commercial return on their nominal amount
or which is determined by reference to the results of a business or the value of any property; and they do not
carry a right on repayment to an amount which exceeds their nominal amount and is not reasonably comparable
with what is generally repayable in respect of similar loan capital listed on the Official List of the FCA.

No United Kingdom stamp duty should be payable on the sale of the Loans by the Seller to the Issuer, on the
assignment of the related rights to the Issuer or on the grant of security by the Issuer to the Trustee,
because stamp duty is now chargeable only on instruments transferring stock or marketable securities, and the
Loans and the related rights are neither. No stamp duty land tax, land and buildings transaction tax or land
transaction tax arises, because no interest in land is transferred.

## Taxation of the Issuer

### The securitisation company regime

The Issuer expects to be a "note-issuing company" within the meaning of the Taxation of Securitisation
Companies Regulations 2006 (SI 2006/3296) (the "Securitisation Company Regulations"), and therefore a
"securitisation company" for the purposes of those regulations. A securitisation company is taxed on the basis
of its "retained profit", that is, broadly, the amount of profit which the Transaction Documents provide that
it is to retain for its own benefit in each accounting period, and not on the profits shown in its accounts.
The amounts which the Issuer receives and pays under the Transaction Documents, including timing differences
between its accounting profits and its cash flows and any impairment of the Loans, are disregarded in computing
its taxable profits, except to the extent of its retained profit. The Transaction Documents provide for the
Issuer to retain a small amount of profit in each accounting period, and the Issuer expects that its liability
to United Kingdom corporation tax will be limited to tax on that retained profit at the prevailing rate.

The application of the Securitisation Company Regulations depends on the Issuer continuing to satisfy their
conditions, which include conditions as to the nature of its business and its assets, the issue of capital
market investments as part of a capital market arrangement, the payments which it makes and receives, and the
absence of any arrangement of which the main purpose, or one of the main purposes, is to secure a tax advantage
for any person. The Issuer has covenanted in Condition 4 (*Issuer covenants*) not to take any action that would
cause it to cease to be a securitisation company. If the Issuer ceased to be a securitisation company, it could
be taxed on its accounting profits, which in a given accounting period could exceed its cash income and its
retained profit, and the tax payable would reduce the amounts available to pay the Noteholders. Such an event
may entitle the Issuer to redeem the Notes under Condition 6(e).

### Holdings

Holdings is not expected to be a securitisation company. It will be subject to United Kingdom corporation tax on
its income and gains under the general rules. Holdings is not expected to have any significant income, and any
tax payable by it will not be an obligation of the Issuer.

### Value added tax

Supplies of services to the Issuer under the Transaction Documents may be subject to United Kingdom value added
tax ("VAT") at the standard rate, depending on their nature. The Issuer is not expected to be able to recover
input VAT, because its supplies are exempt or outside the scope of VAT. Any irrecoverable VAT on the fees of the
Servicer, the Cash Manager, the Trustee or any other service provider will be an expense of the Issuer and will
reduce the funds available to pay the Noteholders. A change in the VAT treatment of the servicing of the Loans
could increase that expense.

## Other tax considerations

### Noteholders resident in the United Kingdom

A Noteholder within the charge to United Kingdom corporation tax will generally be taxed on the return on the
Notes, including interest, profits on disposal and exchange gains or losses, as income in accordance with the
loan relationship rules in Part 5 of the Corporation Tax Act 2009, broadly by reference to the amounts
recognised in its accounts under generally accepted accounting practice. The Notes are offered only to
professional investors, and this summary does not describe the position of individual Noteholders.

### Noteholders not resident in the United Kingdom

Interest on the Notes has a United Kingdom source and may therefore be chargeable to United Kingdom tax by direct
assessment even where it is paid without deduction. However, a Noteholder that is not resident in the United
Kingdom and does not hold the Notes in connection with a trade carried on in the United Kingdom through a
permanent establishment, branch or agency will generally have no liability to United Kingdom tax on the interest
beyond any tax deducted at source.

### The proposed financial transaction tax

The European Commission's proposal for a financial transaction tax to be adopted by certain participating member
states of the European Union under the enhanced co-operation procedure has not been adopted. The United Kingdom
is not a participating state. Prospective Noteholders should seek their own advice on any financial transaction
tax that may apply to them in any other jurisdiction.

### Final statement

This section is a general summary only. It is not a complete analysis of the tax consequences of acquiring,
holding or disposing of the Notes, it is based on the law and practice in force at the date of this Prospectus,
and none of the Issuer, the Originator, the Arranger, the Lead Manager and the Trustee accepts responsibility for
the tax consequences for any Noteholder.

# 20. Subscription and sale

## The subscription agreement

Corvane Securities plc (the "Lead Manager") has, under a subscription agreement dated on or about the date of
this Prospectus between the Issuer, the Originator and the Lead Manager (the "Subscription Agreement"), agreed
with the Issuer, subject to the satisfaction of the conditions set out in it, to subscribe or procure
subscribers for the Notes on the Closing Date at an issue price of 100% of their initial principal amount. The
Originator has agreed in the Subscription Agreement to subscribe for 5% of the initial principal amount of each
class of Notes in its capacity as Retention Holder, as described in section 18, and the Lead Manager's
obligation applies to the remaining Notes of each class.

The obligations of the Lead Manager under the Subscription Agreement are subject to conditions precedent that
include: (a) the execution and delivery of the Transaction Documents by each party to them on or before the
Closing Date; (b) the delivery of legal opinions from Hartwell Quinn LLP and Grantley Moss LLP and of
certificates from the directors of the Issuer and of the Originator, in a form satisfactory to the Lead
Manager; (c) the assignment of the ratings described in section 4 to the Notes by the Rating Agencies, and the
absence of any downgrade or announcement of a possible downgrade before the Closing Date; (d) the accuracy of
the representations and warranties of the Issuer and the Originator on the date of the Subscription Agreement
and on the Closing Date; and (e) the absence, before the Closing Date, of any material adverse change in the
financial condition or prospects of the Issuer or the Originator, or of any change in national or
international financial, political or economic conditions which in the opinion of the Lead Manager would be
likely to prejudice materially the success of the offering of the Notes.

The Issuer and the Originator have given representations and warranties to the Lead Manager about themselves,
the Loans, the Transaction Documents and the information contained in this Prospectus. The Originator has
agreed to indemnify the Lead Manager against certain liabilities incurred in connection with the issue of the
Notes, and to pay the fees, commissions and expenses of the Arranger and the Lead Manager and the other costs
of the transaction, as described in section 6. The Lead Manager is entitled to terminate the Subscription
Agreement in certain circumstances before payment for the Notes is made to the Issuer, including a breach of
warranty, the failure of a condition precedent or a material change in market conditions.

Settlement of the Notes will take place on the Closing Date on a delivery-versus-payment basis through the
Clearing Systems, as described in section 21. The Issuer will not receive any proceeds from the Notes until
the Closing Date, and it will apply the proceeds as described in section 6.

## Selling restrictions: general

The Lead Manager has represented, warranted and agreed that it has complied and will comply with all
applicable laws and regulations in each country or jurisdiction in or from which it purchases, offers, sells
or delivers Notes, or possesses, distributes or publishes this Prospectus or any other offering material
relating to the Notes, in all cases at its own expense. The Lead Manager will obtain any consent, approval or
permission required by it for the purchase, offer, sale or delivery of the Notes under the laws and
regulations in force in any jurisdiction to which it is subject or in which it makes such purchases, offers,
sales or deliveries.

No action has been or will be taken by the Issuer, the Originator, the Arranger or the Lead Manager that
would, or is intended to, permit a public offer of the Notes, or the possession, circulation or distribution
of this Prospectus or of any other material relating to the Issuer, the Originator or the Notes, in any
country or jurisdiction where action for that purpose is required. Accordingly, the Notes may not be offered
or sold, directly or indirectly, and neither this Prospectus nor any other offering material or advertisement
in connection with the Notes may be distributed or published, in or from any country or jurisdiction except in
circumstances that will result in compliance with all applicable laws and regulations.

cause to be communicated, an invitation or inducement to engage in investment activity (within the meaning of
section 21 of FSMA) received by it in connection with the issue or sale of any Notes in circumstances in
which section 21(1) of FSMA does not apply to the Issuer;

(b) *General compliance*: it has complied and will comply with all applicable provisions of FSMA and of
the rules and regulations made under it with respect to anything done by it in relation to the Notes in, from
or otherwise involving the United Kingdom;

The Notes have not been and will not be registered under the Securities Act or the securities laws of any

registration requirements of the Securities Act. The Lead Manager has represented, warranted and agreed that:

as part of its distribution at any time and otherwise until 40 days after the later of the commencement of the
offering and the Closing Date (the "distribution compliance period");

(b) *No directed selling efforts*: neither it, nor its affiliates, nor any person acting on its or their
behalf has engaged or will engage in any directed selling efforts with respect to the Notes, and it and they
have complied and will comply with the offering restrictions requirement of Regulation S;

(c) *Confirmations*: it will have sent to each distributor, dealer or other person to which it sells Notes
during the distribution compliance period a confirmation or other notice setting out the restrictions on
offers and sales of the Notes within the United States or to, or for the account or benefit of, U.S. persons;
and

(d) *Risk retention*: it will not sell Notes to, or for the account or benefit of, any person that is a "U.S.
person" as defined in the credit risk retention regulations issued under Section 15G of the U.S. Securities
Exchange Act of 1934, as amended (the "U.S. Risk Retention Rules"), without the prior written consent of the
Originator.

The transaction is not intended to involve the retention by a sponsor of a credit risk under the U.S. Risk
Retention Rules. The Issuer intends to rely on the exemption for non-U.S. transactions in Section 20 of the
U.S. Risk Retention Rules, which requires, among other conditions, that no more than 10% of the dollar value of
all classes of interests issued in the securitisation be sold or transferred to, or for the account or benefit
of, persons that are U.S. persons for the purposes of those rules. Each purchaser of Notes will be deemed to
represent that it is not such a U.S. person, or that it has obtained the consent of the Originator.

The Issuer has not been and will not be registered under the U.S. Investment Company Act of 1940, as amended.
Terms used in this paragraph and not otherwise defined have the meanings given to them by Regulation S.

## Prohibition of sales to retail investors

For the avoidance of doubt, the Notes are offered only to professional investors and eligible counterparties.
No Notes may be offered, sold or otherwise made available to any retail investor in the United Kingdom or the
EEA, whether in the initial distribution or in any subsequent resale, and no key information document has been
prepared for the Notes under the UK or EEA rules described in the Important notices. Each purchaser of Notes
will be deemed to represent and agree that it is not a retail investor in the United Kingdom or the EEA and
that it will not offer, sell or otherwise make available any Note to such a person. Any Distributor that
resells the Notes is responsible for its own compliance with the product governance and retail distribution
rules that apply to it.

## Other jurisdictions

No action has been taken to permit an offer of the Notes in any jurisdiction other than the United Kingdom and
the member states of the EEA, and then only to the persons and in the circumstances described above. The Lead
Manager has represented, warranted and agreed that it will not offer or sell any Notes, or distribute this
Prospectus, in any other jurisdiction except in circumstances that will result in compliance with the
applicable laws and regulations of that jurisdiction and that will not impose any obligation on the Issuer,
the Originator or the Arranger.

## General

Neither the Issuer nor the Lead Manager represents that the Notes may at any time lawfully be sold in
compliance with any applicable registration or other requirements in any jurisdiction, or under any exemption
available in it, or assumes any responsibility for facilitating any such sale. The selling restrictions in this
section may be modified by agreement between the Issuer and the Lead Manager following a change in a relevant
law, regulation or directive, or in its interpretation. Any such modification will be set out in a supplement
to this Prospectus.

Each person who purchases Notes in the secondary market will be deemed to have made the representations and
agreements in this section that apply to it, as if references to the Lead Manager were references to that
person. Persons into whose hands this Prospectus comes are required by the Issuer and the Lead Manager to
comply with all applicable laws and regulations in each country or jurisdiction in which they purchase, offer,
sell or deliver Notes or possess, distribute or publish this Prospectus or any other offering material, in all
cases at their own expense.

The Lead Manager and its affiliates have engaged, and may in the future engage, in investment banking,
commercial banking and other dealings in the ordinary course of business with Kestrel Group plc, the Originator
and their affiliates, and may receive customary fees and commissions for them. In the ordinary course of their
business activities, the Lead Manager and its affiliates may make or hold a broad array of investments,
including in the Notes, and may actively trade debt and equity securities for their own account and for the
accounts of their customers. Such investment and trading activities may involve securities of the Issuer or of
the Originator. The Lead Manager has no obligation to make a market in the Notes.

\newpage

# 21. General information

## Authorisation

The issue of the Notes, and the entry by the Issuer into the Transaction Documents to which it is a party, were
authorised by resolutions of the board of directors of the Issuer passed on 19 January 2026. The Issuer has
obtained all necessary consents, approvals and authorisations in the United Kingdom in connection with the
issue and performance of the Notes. The entry by Holdings into the deed of covenant described in section 7 was
authorised by a resolution of its board of directors passed on the same date. The entry by the Originator into
the Transaction Documents to which it is a party was authorised by a resolution of its board of directors.

## Litigation

The Issuer is not, and has not been since its incorporation, involved in any governmental, legal or arbitration
proceedings (including any such proceedings which are pending or threatened of which the Issuer is aware) which
may have, or have had, a significant effect on the financial position or profitability of the Issuer. The
same statement is made by Holdings in respect of itself.

The Originator is, in the ordinary course of its business, party to complaints and claims by its customers,
including complaints referred to the Financial Ombudsman Service, and is affected by the review of
historical motor finance commission arrangements carried out by the FCA. The effect of these matters on the
Loans and the Notes, and the risks they present, are described in sections 2 and 8.

## No significant change

Since the date of its incorporation, there has been no material adverse change in the financial position or
prospects of the Issuer and no significant change in its financial or trading position. Since the date of its
incorporation, the Issuer has not entered into any contract or arrangement, other than in the ordinary course
of its business, and has not engaged in any activity, other than as described in section 7.

## Accounts

No statutory accounts have been prepared for the Issuer or for Holdings as at the date of this Prospectus. The
first financial period of each of them will end on 31 December 2026. The Issuer will publish its audited
annual accounts within the period required by the Companies Act 2006 and will deliver copies of them to the
Trustee and to the Principal Paying Agent. Fennick & Rowe LLP have been appointed as the Auditors, as described
in section 7. The Issuer does not intend to publish interim accounts.

## Documents available for inspection

For so long as any Note is outstanding, copies of the following documents may be inspected in electronic form,
on request, during usual business hours on any weekday (public holidays excepted) at the registered office of
the Issuer and at the specified office of the Principal Paying Agent, and will be made available on the
securitisation repository website described below:

(a) the certificate of incorporation, the certificate entitling the Issuer to do business as a public company,
and the memorandum and articles of association of the Issuer and of Holdings;

(b) the annual audited accounts of the Issuer and of Holdings, when published;

(c) the Trust Deed, which contains the forms of the global Notes and the Conditions (section 5);

(d) the Deed of Charge;

(e) the Sale Agreement, and the declaration of trust in favour of the Issuer over the Loans whose Borrowers
reside in Scotland (the Scottish Declaration of Trust);

(f) the declaration of trust by Kestrel Motor Finance Ltd over the Collection Account (the "Collection Account
Declaration of Trust");

(g) the Servicing Agreement;

(h) the Cash Management Agreement;

(i) the Bank Account Agreement;

(j) the Agency Agreement;

(k) the Corporate Services Agreement and the Share Trust Deed;

(l) the master definitions schedule, which sets out the definitions that apply to all the Transaction
Documents (the "Master Schedule"); and

(m) the Subscription Agreement, and each investor report published under the Cash Management Agreement.

The documents in paragraphs (c) to (l) are governed by English law, save for the Scottish Declaration of Trust,
which is governed by Scots law.

## Clearing and settlement

The Notes have been accepted for clearance through the international central securities depositaries selected
by the Issuer (the "Clearing Systems"). Each class of Notes is issued in global registered form, in minimum
denominations of £100,000 and integral multiples of £1,000 above that amount, and is represented by a global
note registered in the name of a nominee of a common safekeeper for the Clearing Systems. Definitive Notes will
be issued only in the limited circumstances set out in the Conditions (section 5). The identification codes of
the Notes are:

| Class | ISIN | Common code |
|---|---|---|
| Class A Notes | XS0000000001 | 000000001 |
| Class B Notes | XS0000000002 | 000000002 |
| Class C Notes | XS0000000003 | 000000003 |
| Class D Notes | XS0000000004 | 000000004 |

Payments on the Notes will be made by the Principal Paying Agent to the Clearing Systems for credit to the
accounts of their participants, and investors will hold interests in the Notes through those participants. The
Issuer will have no responsibility for the records of the Clearing Systems or their participants. The Legal
Entity Identifier of the Issuer is 00000000THAMES2026A1.

## Investor reporting

The Cash Manager will prepare, on behalf of the Issuer, a monthly investor report for each period, using the
information on the Loans provided by the Servicer. The content of the investor report, the date by which it is
published and the tests it reports are described in section 16. The investor report will be made available to
the Noteholders, the Trustee, the Rating Agencies and, on request, to potential investors in the Notes.

## Post-issuance reporting under the UK securitisation framework

The Issuer has been designated, in accordance with the Securitisation Regulations 2024 and SECN 6 of the FCA
Handbook, as the entity responsible for fulfilling the transparency requirements of the UK securitisation
framework (the "Reporting Entity"), and the Originator has agreed to provide it with all the information it
needs to do so. The Reporting Entity will make the following information available to the holders of the
Notes, the competent authorities and, on request, potential investors, by means of a securitisation
repository or, if none is available, a website that meets the requirements of SECN 6:

(a) before pricing and on the Closing Date, the Transaction Documents, this Prospectus and the loan-level
information on the Loans, in each case in the form required by SECN 6;

(b) on a monthly basis, loan-level information on the Loans in the template required by SECN 6, published at
the same time as the investor report;

(c) on a monthly basis, an investor report in the form required by SECN 6, covering the performance of the
Loans, the cash flows and the application of funds, the trigger events, and the Retention Holder's
confirmation of its continued holding of the vertical slice described in section 18; and

(d) without delay, any significant event affecting the transaction, including a
material breach of the Transaction Documents, a change in the structural features or the risk characteristics
of the transaction, the occurrence of a trigger event, the termination of the appointment of the Servicer, and
any material amendment to the Transaction Documents.

The Issuer will publish each item of information described above within the periods set by SECN 6 and will
keep it available for at least five years after the final maturity date of the Notes (20 December 2032).
The risk retention commitment of the Retention Holder and the other regulatory disclosures that apply to the
transaction are described in section 18.

## Interests of persons involved in the issue

Save for the fees payable to the Arranger and the Lead Manager, the fees payable to the transaction parties
under the Transaction Documents, the Deferred Consideration payable to the Seller and the Notes held by the
Originator as Retention Holder, so far as the Issuer is aware no person involved in the issue of the Notes has
an interest material to the offer. The capacities in which each party acts, and the conflicts of interest
which may arise from them, are described in sections 2 and 3.

## Expenses of the issue

The expenses incurred in connection with the issue of the Notes, including the fees of the Arranger and the
Lead Manager, the legal advisers, the Auditors and the Rating Agencies, will be paid by the Originator and not
by the Issuer, as described in section 6. The servicer fee and the trustee fee are paid by the Issuer as
senior fees in the Priority of Payments in section 15; the fees of the Cash Manager, the Account Bank,
the Collection Account Bank, the Agent Bank, the Principal Paying Agent and the Back-up Servicer Facilitator
are paid by the Servicer out of the servicer fee.

## Information from third parties

Where information in this Prospectus has been sourced from a third party, including the information on the
United Kingdom motor finance market and on used vehicle prices in sections 2 and 8, the Issuer confirms that
the information has been accurately reproduced and that, as far as the Issuer is aware and is able to
ascertain from information published by that third party, no facts have been omitted which would render the
reproduced information inaccurate or misleading. The Issuer has not independently verified any such
information.

## Post-issuance information

Save for the investor reports and the information described above under *Post-issuance reporting under the UK
securitisation framework*, and the notices to Noteholders required by the Conditions (section 5), the Issuer
does not intend to provide any post-issuance information in relation to the Notes or the Loans.

## Websites

Websites referred to in this Prospectus do not form part of this Prospectus, and the information on them has
not been scrutinised or approved by any authority. The loan-level information and the investor reports are
made available for the purposes of the UK securitisation framework only.

## Governing law

The Notes, the Conditions and the Transaction Documents, and any non-contractual obligations arising out of or
in connection with them, are governed by English law, save as stated above for the Scottish Declaration of
Trust. The courts of England have exclusive jurisdiction to settle any dispute arising out of or in connection
with the Notes.
